parameters {
   /* 
       Jump compocurrent parameters on GBM sde (JUMP)
   */ 
   real mu_j;                // Structural mean (location) of the jump size
   real<lower=0> sigma_j;    // Standard deviation (scale) of the jump variability
   vector[N] jump_std;       // Latent jump innovations (standardized N(0,1) for non-centered parameterization)  

   /* 
       Constant drift rate representing the long-term expected asset returns
	                           (geometric growth)
   */						   
   real mu_mean;
   
   /*
                       ---- Base mean-reversion speed : ----
						  
     Measures the intensity of the elastic correction force applied to the 
     absolute price deviation (in pips) relative to the EMA attractor.
     Works coupled with 'ou_shape' to define the aggressiveness of the non-linear 
     adjustment. Restricted to positive values (>0) to ensure process stability.
  */
   real<lower=0> phi_reversion;
  /*
      
                       ---- Ema distance multiplier (beta) dynamics : ----
   
     The beta parameter functions as an amplification or adjustment factor 
     over the distance between the two EMAs:
   
        - If beta = 0: The target price is purely the Fast EMA.
        - If beta > 0: The target price is pushed in the direction of the trend (momentum).
        - If beta < 0: The target price is pulled back (a sort of compression prior to the reversion).
   
   */
   real beta;
   /*
   
                       ---- Non-linear reversion curvature (OU_SHAPE): ----
					   
					   
     Governs the elasticity and geometry of the mean-reversion force.
     
	    - If ou_shape = 1: The force scales linearly with the price deviation.
        - If ou_shape > 1: The force becomes convex (accelerates rapidly at large deviations).
        - If ou_shape < 1: The force becomes concave (acts strongly on small deviations but dampens out).
     
	 Restricted to positive values (>0) to maintain directional stability.
   
   */
   real<lower=0> ou_shape;
   /*
     EXOGENOUS LIQUIDITY COEFFICIENTS (ABSOLUTE SCALE):
     gamma_grad:  Measures the direct nominal impact (in pips) of the pure liquidity gradient.
     gamma_vspri: Measures the direct nominal impact (in pips) of liquidity coupled with price movement.
     Both act as linear additive biases shifting the conditional mean independently of the price level.
   */
   real gamma_grad;
   real gamma_vspri;
   // Baseline probability of a jump (logit scale)
   real alpha_jump;

   // Effects on jump probability (logit model)
   real beta_breakout;  // breakout events increase jump likelihood
   real beta_near_sr;   // proximity to support/resistance (compression)
   real beta_ema;       // EMA pressure / trend structure effect
   
   // Day-of-week fixed effects (shared across regimes)
   vector[5] beta_dow;
  
   /*    Uncentralized correalation between Log-Volatility and log-returns   */
   real <lower=0.0, upper=1.0>  rho_raw;   
   /*  
                        ---- MomentumRNN hyperparameters: ----
     momentum (alpha): Controls inertia of momentum vectors (v_g_o, v_n_d, etc.).
     A value of 0.90 is common in neural networks, smoothing updates.
     Higher values (closer to 1) mean more inertia.
   */ 
   real<lower=0.0,upper=1.0> momentum;
   /*
     epsilon (eta): Controls the influence of current volatility innovations (n[t-1])
     on the momentum vectors. Often analogous to a learning rate.
   */
   real<lower=0,upper=1> epsilon;
   /*   Markov Switching Regime   */
   vector[2] mu_1_state;                  // Regime-dependent volatility levels
   /*
                 ---- Coefficient for the mean of `n` (log-volatility innovation): ----
       This one models a cross-equation non-linearity, relating past returns and volatility
       to the current volatility innovation. This is a key part of the MomentumRNN integration.
      `beta10`: coefficient for the `h` (LSTM hidden state) term in the `n` innovation mean.

   */
   vector[2] beta10_state;
   // Macro news impact on volatility by regime
   
   vector <lower=0> [2] beta_usa_state;   // US macro events (USD-driven shocks)
   vector <lower=0> [2] beta_eur_state;   // Eurozone macro events (EUR-driven shocks)
   vector <lower=0> [2] gamma_geo_state;  // Geopolitical risk amplification
   
   /*   
     ---- Volatility of the log-volatility innovations (n). ----
      Controls the magnitude of the random shocks to volatility.
   */
   vector <lower=0.0001> [2]  tau_state;  // standard deviation of the log-volatility innovation
   /*
   
                 ---- Hidden Markov Model (HMM) Transition Probabilities ----
     Probability of remaining in the current state (e.g., state 1 to state 1, or state 2 to state 2).
     Implicitly, 1 - p_remain[state] is the probability of transitioning to the other state.
	 
   */
   vector <lower=0.01, upper=0.99>[2] p_remain;  // Regime persistence
   /* 
      ---- Regime switching mean Innovations on log-volatility (`n`) ----
              `n` is a vector of innovations for times t=1 to N.
   */
   vector [N] n; 
   //
   /*  
      ---- Scaled LSTM  Gate Parameters (MomentumRNN-like) ----
      These are scalar versions of the weights for the various gates and cell states,
      multiplied by epsilon and used in the momentum update step. 
   */
   // ===========================================================
   //                LSTM parameters
   // ===========================================================
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_d_scalar;    // Candidate cell state (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_d_scalar;    // Candidate cell state (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_i_scalar;    // Input gate (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_i_scalar;    // Input gate (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thetv_o_scalar;   // Output gate (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_o_scalar;    // Output gate (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_f_scalar;    // Forget gate (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_f_scalar;    // Forget gate (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_d_scalar;    // Bias for candidate cell state
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_i_scalar;    // Bias for input gate
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_o_scalar;    // Bias for output gate
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_f_scalar;    // Bias for forget gate
   /*  
      ---- Scaled  GRU Gate Parameters (MomentumRNN-like) ----
      These are scalar versions of the weights for the various gates and cell states,
      multiplied by epsilon and used in the momentum update step. 
   */
   //
   /*
   ===========================================================   
    Forward MomentumGRU scalar parameters (Conditional mean)
   ===========================================================
   */
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Forget/Update gate (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Forget/Update gate (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Bias for forget/update gate
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward;   // Candidate hidden state (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward;   // Bias for candidate hidden state
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  theta_h_gru_scalar_forward;  // Candidate hidden state (input dependent)
   /*
   ===========================================================
    Backward MomentumGRU scalar parameters (Conditional mean)
   ===========================================================
   */
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_f_gru_scalar_backward;  // The same here too!!!  
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_f_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_f_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_h_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_h_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  theta_h_gru_scalar_backward;
   /* 
             ---- AR2 parameters for z (log-volatility) ----
      These 'rhos' parameters are individually constrained to (-1, 1),
      which guarantees stationarity of the resulting AR coefficients.
   */
   real<lower=-1, upper=1> rho1; // First reflection coefficient
   real<lower=-1, upper=1> rho2; // Second reflection coefficient
   /*      ---- AR1 parameter for exogenous/aux 
                              Parkimson relativity volatility proxy ---- */ 
   real<lower=-1, upper=1> phi_rv;  
   /*        
      ---- LSTM initial States Priors ----
   */ 
   // Initial cell state
   real C_1;
   // Initial hidden state
   real h_1;
   /*  
   =======================================================
      Regime-dependent risk-reward scaling parameters
   =======================================================

     These parameters control the magnitude of Take-Profit (TP) and Stop-Loss (SL)
     levels as multiples of tail-based risk measures (VaR / Expected Shortfall),
     conditional on the latent market regime.
     
     - Trend regime: allows larger profit targets and wider stops
     - Scalp regime: enforces tighter targets and faster risk control

     They act as a decision layer on top of the predictive distribution,
     translating probabilistic forecasts into executable trading levels.
     
   */
   real<lower=0> k_tp_trend;  // TP scaling factor in trending regime (captures extended moves)
   real<lower=0> k_tp_scalp;  // TP scaling factor in scalping regime (short-term gains)

   real<lower=0> k_sl_trend;  // SL scaling factor in trending regime (absorbs volatility noise)
   real<lower=0> k_sl_scalp;  // SL scaling factor in scalping regime (tight risk control)
       
}   

transformed parameters {
  /* 
       Logit model for jump shocks 
  */
  vector<lower=0.0, upper=1.0>[N] pi_z;
  /* 
       Geometric Brownian Motion with Stochastic Volatility
  */
  vector[N] sigma_s;        // Standard deviation of volatility (physical scale)
  vector[N] jump;           // Stepped jump compocurrent by "pi_z"
  vector[N] mean_S;         // Conditional mean price (Euler-Maruyama)
  vector[N] sd_S;           // Conditional standard deviation of the price (Euler-Maruyama)
  
  /*
     Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) non-linear drift compocurrent:
     Represents the deterministic attraction force modeled in absolute price terms 
     (raw price/pips). It acts as an additive control adjustment in the conditional 
     mean equation, capturing the state-dependent mean reversion.
  */
  real mu_target_2;
  real deviation_2; 
  real p_chop_2;
  real ou_gate_2;  
  real sign_deviation_2;
  real ou_reversion_2;
  real mu_target;
  real deviation;
  real p_chop; 
  real ou_gate;
  real sign_deviation;
  real ou_reversion;
  vector[N] ou_drift; 
  // 
  real<lower=-1,upper=1> rho; // Correlation between log-returns and log-volatility innovations    
  vector [N] mu_regime;
  vector [N] beta10_regime; 
  vector [N] tau_regime; 
  vector [N] beta_usa_regime;
  vector [N] beta_eur_regime;
  vector [N] gamma_geo_regime;   
  /* 
       SV-AR 2 coefficients (WANG, 2016, p. 63, Model 3)
  */
  real phi;
  real psi;
  /* 
      `z`: Latent log-volatility process. 
      This is the core state variable for volatility in the SV model.
  */
  vector [N] z;
  /*  
       ---- LSTM vectors declaration ---- 
  */
  // LSTM hidden state
  vector [N] h;
  // LSTM cell state
  vector [N] C;
  // LSTM candidate cell state
  vector <lower=-1.0, upper=1.0> [N] n_d;
  // LSTM input gate
  vector <lower= 0.0, upper=1.0> [N] g_i;
  // LSTM output gate
  vector <lower= 0.0, upper=1.0> [N] g_o;
  // LSTM forget gate
  vector <lower= 0.0, upper=1.0> [N] g_f;
  // LSTM momentum for output gate
  vector [N] v_g_o;
  // LSTM momentum for candidate cell state
  vector [N] v_n_d;
  // LSTM momentum for input gate
  vector [N] v_g_i;
  // LSTM momentum for forget gate
  vector [N] v_g_f;
  //
  /* 
         ---- Accumulator for HMM Forward Algorithm ----
     Used to sum log-probabilities for the HMM forward algorithm,
	 helping to avoid numerical underflow.
  */
  real accumulator[2];
  real mu;
  matrix[N, 2] log_alpha;   // Forward log-probabilities
  vector[2] mu_1_state_sorted;
  vector[2] beta10_state_sorted;
  vector[2] tau_state_sorted;
  vector[2] beta_usa_state_sorted;
  vector[2] beta_eur_state_sorted;
  vector[2] gamma_geo_state_sorted;   
  /*
	    ---- Hidden Markov Model (HMM) Transition Matrix ----
    'A' represents the transition probabilities between the two hidden states (e.g., low and high volatility regimes).
     A[i, j] is the probability of transitioning from state 'i' to state 'j'.
     p_remain[s] is the probability of staying in state 's'.
  */
  matrix[2, 2] A;           // Transition matrix
  /*
	   ---- HMM Log-Likelihood for Each State ----
   log_A[t, s] stores the log of the joint probability of the observations up to time 't'
   and being in state 's' at time 't'. Used in the forward algorithm for HMM inference. 
  */ 
  matrix[2, 2] log_A;  
  /* 
     'probability' stores the normalized posterior probabilities 
     of being in each state at each time point.
  */
  vector[2] probability[N];
  // 
  /*
           
		   ---- Sorted Regime-Specific Log-Volatility Levels ----

    The original `mu_1_state` and others vectors contain regime-dependent intercepts
    for the log-volatility innovations (n[t]). However, without constraints,
    their labels (e.g., "low" vs. "high" volatility) are exchangeable â€” leading
    to label switching across MCMC chains.

    We sort 'mu_1_state' and others in ascending order to enforce an identifiability constraint,
    ensuring that:
       mu_1_state_sorted[1] < mu_1_state_sorted[2]
    This helps prevent multimodal posteriors and improves mixing in the HMM compocurrent.

    All downstream computations (HMM, mu_regime, forecasts) use the sorted version.
	
  */
  mu_1_state_sorted = sort_asc(mu_1_state);
  beta10_state_sorted  = sort_asc(beta10_state);
  tau_state_sorted = sort_asc(tau_state); 
  beta_usa_state_sorted = sort_asc(beta_usa_state); 
  beta_eur_state_sorted = sort_asc(beta_eur_state); 
  gamma_geo_state_sorted = sort_asc(gamma_geo_state);  
  /*          
         ---- HMM Transition Matrix Initialization ----
     A[1,1] = p_remain[1] means P(State 1 -> State 1)
     A[1,2] = 1 - p_remain[1] means P(State 1 -> State 2)
     A[2,1] = 1 - p_remain[2] means P(State 2 -> State 1)
     A[2,2] = p_remain[2] means P(State 2 -> State 2)
	
  */   
  A[1, 1] = inv_logit(p_remain[1]); A[1, 2] = 1 - inv_logit(p_remain[1]);
  A[2, 1] = 1 - inv_logit(p_remain[2]); A[2, 2] = inv_logit(p_remain[2]);
  for (i in 1:2) {
    for (j in 1:2) { 
	 log_A[i,j] = log(A[i,j]);
	 }
  }
  /*
   ===========================================================
         Transformation Coefficients
   ===========================================================
  
     Derived SV_AR(2) coefficients from reflection coefficients (rho1, rho2)
     This is a standard transformation (e.g., related to the Durbin-Levinson algorithm)
	 Monahan, J.F. (1984), "A Note on Enforcing Stationarity in Autoregressive-Moving Average Models")
  */
  phi = rho1 * (1 - rho2);
  psi = rho2;  
  //
  /*             
               ---- Linear Mapping Transformation ----
        Applies this transformation for leverage coefficient "rho"
  */
  rho = 2*(rho_raw)-1; 
  //
    /*
  =============================
  Jump probability (logistic model)
  =============================
  */ 
  /* 
     pi_z[t] is the probability of a jump at time t.
     It is modeled via a logistic regression combining:
 
         - Price action signals (breakouts, support/resistance proximity, EMA pressure)
         - Calendar effects (day-of-week dummies)
  
    This captures how market conditions and timing influence the likelihood of extreme moves.
  
  */
  for (t in 1:N) {
     pi_z[t] = inv_logit(alpha_jump + beta_breakout * D_breakout[t] + beta_near_sr  * D_near_sr[t] 
	    + beta_ema * D_ema_pressure[t] + beta_dow[1] * date[t,1]
        + beta_dow[2] * date[t,2]
        + beta_dow[3] * date[t,3]
        + beta_dow[4] * date[t,4]
        + beta_dow[5] * date[t,5]);
  }
  /*  
                   ---- Initial LSTM momentum, hidden and cell states values for time t=1 ----
	   
	The first update becomes just the  signal term, which is intuitive and avoids introducing an arbitrary offset. 
	It reduces risk of artificially large gate activations at the start.
	
  */
  v_g_o[1] = 0.0;
  v_n_d[1] = 0.0;
  v_g_i[1] = 0.0;
  v_g_f[1] = 0.0; 
  //  
  /*  
       ---- LSTM momentum update for t=1 (using scalar parameters) ----
  */  
  v_g_o[1] = (momentum * v_g_o[1] + epsilon*thetv_o_scalar*n[1]);
  v_n_d[1] = (momentum * v_n_d[1] + epsilon*thnv_d_scalar*n[1]);
  v_g_i[1] = (momentum * v_g_i[1] + epsilon*thnv_i_scalar*n[1]);
  v_g_f[1] = (momentum * v_g_f[1] + epsilon*thnv_f_scalar*n[1]);
  /*    
       ---- LSTM gate calculations for t=1 ----
  */  
  g_o[1]  = inv_logit(v_g_o[1] + thnw_o_scalar*h_1 + thnb_o_scalar);
  n_d[1]  = tanh(v_n_d[1]+ thnw_d_scalar*h_1 + thnb_d_scalar);
  g_i[1]  = inv_logit(v_g_i[1] + thnw_i_scalar*h_1 + thnb_i_scalar);
  g_f[1]  = inv_logit(v_g_f[1] + thnw_f_scalar*h_1+ thnb_f_scalar);  
  /*   
       ---- LSTM cell and hidden state for t=1 ----
  */
  C[1] =   g_i[1] * n_d[1]  + g_f[1] *C_1;
  h[1] =   g_o[1] * tanh(C[1]); 
  // Expected regime-specific intercept for n[1]
  /*
  
       Here, "smoothing" means the model doesn't commit to a single state at any given moment,
       but instead maintains a probabilistic blend of all possible states. 
       This allows for a more flexible, stable, and realistic representation of how market conditions evolve.
   
  */ 
  //  Initialization at t=1 
  mu_regime[1] = dot_product(mu_1_state_sorted, rep_vector(0.5, 2));
  beta10_regime[1] = dot_product(beta10_state_sorted, rep_vector(0.5, 2));
  tau_regime[1] = dot_product(tau_state_sorted, rep_vector(0.5, 2));
  beta_usa_regime[1] = dot_product(beta_usa_state_sorted ,  rep_vector(0.5, 2));
  beta_eur_regime[1] = dot_product(beta_eur_state_sorted ,  rep_vector(0.5, 2)); 
  gamma_geo_regime[1] =   dot_product(gamma_geo_state_sorted ,  rep_vector(0.5, 2));  
  /*   
      Here, the innovation is the log-volatility eta zero
  */
  z[1] = n[1]  + beta_usa_regime[1]*news_usa[1] +  beta_eur_regime[1] * news_eur[1]
         + gamma_geo_regime[1] * news_geo[1];
  //
  /*  
         ---- HMM Forward Algorithm Initialization for t=1 ----
     Calculates the initial log-probability of being in each state, given the first observation.
     Assumes equal initial state probabilities (0.5).
  */
  for (j in 1:2) {
  
    mu = mu_1_state_sorted[j] + beta_usa_state_sorted[j]*news_usa[1] +  beta_eur_state_sorted[j] * news_eur[1]
         + gamma_geo_state_sorted[j] * news_geo[1]; 
	
    log_alpha[1, j] = log(0.5) + normal_lpdf(z[1] | mu, tau_state_sorted[j]);
  }
  // Convert log-probs to regime probabilities using softmax
  probability[1] = softmax(to_vector(log_alpha[1])); 
  //
  jump[1] = pi_z[1]*(mu_j + sigma_j * jump_std[1]);
  //
  /* 
     Converts the latent log-volatility (z) to the 
	 physical standard deviation scale
  */
  sigma_s[1] = exp(z[1]/2);
  
  /* 
     Euler-Maruyama equation: Raw price average 
	 combining trend (mu) and jumps.
  */
  mean_S[1] = y[1];  //Initial observed price 
  /* 
     Euler-Maruyama diffusion: Scales the local standard deviation 
	 by the price and the square root of time (sqrt(dt)).
  */
  sd_S[1] =  0.00001; // Infinitesimal value solely to avoid uninitialized vectors.
  /*   
       ---- LSTM momentum update for t=2 ----
  */  
  v_g_o[2] = (momentum * v_g_o[1] + epsilon*thetv_o_scalar*n[1]);
  v_n_d[2] = (momentum * v_n_d[1] + epsilon*thnv_d_scalar*n[1]);
  v_g_i[2] = (momentum * v_g_i[1] + epsilon*thnv_i_scalar*n[1]);
  v_g_f[2] = (momentum * v_g_f[1] + epsilon*thnv_f_scalar*n[1]); 
  /*   
       ---- LSTM gate calculations for t=2 ----
  */
  g_o[2]  = inv_logit(v_g_o[2] + thnw_o_scalar*h[1] + thnb_o_scalar);
  n_d[2]  = tanh(v_n_d[2] + thnw_d_scalar*h[1] + thnb_d_scalar);
  g_i[2]  = inv_logit(v_g_i[2] + thnw_i_scalar*h[1] + thnb_i_scalar);
  g_f[2]  = inv_logit(v_g_f[2] + thnw_f_scalar*h[1] + thnb_f_scalar); 
  /*   
       ---- LSTM cell and hidden state for t=2 ----
  */	   
  C[2] =   g_i[2] *n_d[2]  + g_f[2] *C[1];
  h[2] =   g_o[2] * tanh(C[2]); 
  /*   
       ---- AR 1 compocurrent at time = 2, with regime shift on eta[2] ----
  */
  
  // ---- Compute expected regime (Bayesian filtered) ----
  
  mu_regime[2] = dot_product(mu_1_state_sorted, probability[1]);
  beta10_regime[2] = dot_product(beta10_state_sorted, probability[1]);
  tau_regime[2] = dot_product(tau_state_sorted, probability[1]);
  beta_usa_regime[2] = dot_product(beta_usa_state_sorted ,  probability[1]);
  beta_eur_regime[2] = dot_product(beta_eur_state_sorted ,  probability[1]); 
  gamma_geo_regime[2] = dot_product(gamma_geo_state_sorted ,  probability[1]);
  
  // ---- Generate log-volatility z[2] with innovation n[2]  ----
  z[2] = n[2] + phi*(z[1]) + phi_rv*(rv[1]) +  beta_usa_regime[2]*news_usa[2]
         +  beta_eur_regime[2] * news_eur[2] + gamma_geo_regime[2] * news_geo[2];
  //  
  /*   
  
         ---- HMM Forward Algorithm  for t=2 ----
		 
      At time t = 2, the volatility innovation `n[2]` is drawn from a
      regime-weighted expectation (mu_regime[2] and others in volatility innovations eq)
	  based on the filtered state probabilities at t = 1.

      The forward algorithm then computes the regime-specific likelihood
      of the observed volatility state z[2] given each possible transition
      from prior states (i -> j), using the regime-specific mean.

      This maintains consistency with the Bayesian HMM structure and
      enables accurate posterior regime probabilities.
	  
  */
  for (j in 1:2) { // current state t=2
   for (i in 1:2) { // previous state t=1
   
     mu = mu_1_state_sorted[j] + beta10_state_sorted[j] * h[1];
	 
     mu += phi * z[1] + phi_rv*rv[1]  + beta_usa_state_sorted[j]*news_usa[2]
         +  beta_eur_state_sorted[j] * news_eur[2] + gamma_geo_state_sorted[j] * news_geo[2];	//  mean for z[2]
	 
     accumulator[i] = log_alpha[1, i] + log_A[i, j] + normal_lpdf(z[2] | mu, tau_state_sorted[j]);	 	              
   }
   log_alpha[2, j] = log_sum_exp(accumulator);
  }
  probability[2] = softmax(to_vector(log_alpha[2]));
  //
  jump[2] = pi_z[2] *(mu_j + sigma_j * jump_std[2]);
  //
  sigma_s[2] = exp(z[2]/2);
  
  /*
            ---- Price-Level Dynamic Attractor ----
          
     The target of attraction is the Fast EMA price, shifted by the distance
     from the Slow EMA via the beta parameter. Unit: Raw price (e.g., 1.0850).
  */
  mu_target_2 = EMA_fast[1] + beta * (EMA_fast[1] - EMA_medium[1]);

  /*
            ---- Absolute Scale Deviation (Price) ----
          
     Measures the direct distance between the previous closing price y[1]
     and our dynamic attractor mu_target_2.
  */
  deviation_2 = y[1] - mu_target_2;

  /*
            ---- HMM-Based Modulation (Smooth Threshold) ----
          
     Extracts the probability of the consolidation/chop state (HMM State 2).
     ou_gate ranges from 0 (reversion fully off) to 1 (reversion fully active).
  */
  p_chop_2 = probability[1, 2]; 
  ou_gate_2 = fmin(fmax((p_chop_2 - 0.6) / 0.4, 0.0), 1.0);

  /*
            ---- Non-Linear Reversion Force  ----
          
     If deviation > 0 (price above the attractor), the force pushes downward.
     The ou_shape parameter dictates whether this pull is convex (>1) or concave (<1).
  */
  sign_deviation_2 = deviation_2 > 0 ? 1.0 : -1.0;
  ou_reversion_2 = - ou_gate_2 * phi_reversion * (pow(fabs(deviation_2) + 1e-6, ou_shape) * sign_deviation_2);

  /*

            ---- Integrated Drift in Brownian Motion  ----

     Since ou_reversion is expressed on an absolute price scale and inherently accounts for direction, it is directly assigned to ou_drift. 
     No scaling by y[1] is required, as the dimensions are already consistent with the asset's nominal price units.
  */
  ou_drift[2] = ou_reversion_2;
  
  // Update conditional mean and standard deviation for t=2
  mean_S[2] = y[1] + (mu_mean * y[1] * dt) +  (gamma_grad * liquidity_grad_t[1] * dt) + 
              (gamma_vspri * scalp_index_t[1] * dt) + (ou_drift[2] * dt) + (jump[1] * y[1] * dt);
			  
  sd_S[2] =   sigma_s[2]* y[1] * sqrt(dt);
  //
  for (t in 3:N){  
	/*   
	     ---- LSTM momentum update for t ---- 
    */		 
    v_g_o[t] = (momentum * v_g_o[t-1] + epsilon*thetv_o_scalar*n[t-1]);
    v_n_d[t] = (momentum * v_n_d[t-1] + epsilon*thnv_d_scalar*n[t-1]);
    v_g_i[t] = (momentum * v_g_i[t-1] + epsilon*thnv_i_scalar*n[t-1]);
    v_g_f[t] = (momentum * v_g_f[t-1] + epsilon*thnv_f_scalar*n[t-1]);
	/*  
         ---- LSTM gate calculations for t ----
	*/
    g_o[t]  = inv_logit(v_g_o[t] + thnw_o_scalar*h[t-1] + thnb_o_scalar);
	n_d[t]  = tanh(v_n_d[t] + thnw_d_scalar*h[t-1] + thnb_d_scalar);
    g_i[t]  = inv_logit(v_g_i[t] + thnw_i_scalar*h[t-1] + thnb_i_scalar);
	g_f[t]  = inv_logit(v_g_f[t] + thnw_f_scalar*h[t-1] + thnb_f_scalar);  
	/*
	     ---- LSTM cell and hidden state for t ----
	*/		 
    C[t] =   g_i[t] *n_d[t]  + g_f[t] *C[t-1];
    h[t] =   g_o[t] * tanh(C[t]); 
    
	/*   ---- Markov Switching terms for t ----  */
	
	mu_regime[t] = dot_product(mu_1_state_sorted, probability[t-1]);
	beta10_regime[t] = dot_product(beta10_state_sorted, probability[t-1]);
	tau_regime[t] = dot_product(tau_state_sorted, probability[t-1]);
    beta_usa_regime[t] = dot_product(beta_usa_state_sorted ,  probability[t-1]);
    beta_eur_regime[t] = dot_product(beta_eur_state_sorted ,  probability[t-1]); 
    gamma_geo_regime[t] = dot_product(gamma_geo_state_sorted ,  probability[t-1]);;
	
	// AR(2) evolution of latent log-volatility with innovation n[t] 
	z[t] = n[t] + phi*(z[t-1])+ psi*(z[t-2]) + phi_rv*(rv[t-1]) +  beta_usa_regime[t]*news_usa[t]
           +  beta_eur_regime[t] * news_eur[t] + gamma_geo_regime[t] * news_geo[t];
	//
    for (j in 1:2) { // current state
      for (i in 1:2) { // Previous state
	  
        mu = mu_1_state_sorted[j] +  beta10_state_sorted[j] * h[t - 1]
             + rho * tau_state_sorted[j] * exp(-0.5 * z[t - 1]) * log(y[t - 1]/y[t - 2]);
			 
		mu += phi * z[t - 1] + psi * z[t - 2]  + phi_rv*rv[t-1]  
		      + beta_usa_state_sorted[j]*news_usa[t] +  beta_eur_state_sorted[j] * news_eur[t]
			  + gamma_geo_state_sorted[j] * news_geo[t];	
		
        accumulator[i] = log_alpha[t - 1, i]  // Log-probability from previous observation and state i
		                 
						 + log_A[i, j]  // Log-transition probability from i to j
						 
						 // Log-likelihood of volatility given previous state i's volatility
						 + normal_lpdf(z[t] | mu, tau_state_sorted[j]);
	    }
      log_alpha[t, j] = log_sum_exp(accumulator); // Sums log-probabilities for all paths leading to state j
 	  } 
	  probability[t] = softmax(to_vector(log_alpha[t])); // Normalizes to get posterior probabilities for current time 't'.
    //
	jump[t] = pi_z[t] *(mu_j + sigma_j * jump_std[t]);
    //
	sigma_s[t] = exp(z[t]/2);
	
    mu_target = EMA_fast[t-1] + beta * (EMA_fast[t-1] - EMA_medium[t-1]);
    deviation = y[t-1] - mu_target;

    p_chop = probability[t-1, 2]; 
    ou_gate = fmin(fmax((p_chop - 0.6) / 0.4, 0.0), 1.0);

    sign_deviation = deviation > 0 ? 1.0 : -1.0;
    ou_reversion = - ou_gate * phi_reversion * (pow(fabs(deviation) + 1e-6, ou_shape) * sign_deviation);

    ou_drift[t] = ou_reversion;
	
	mean_S[t] = y[t-1] + (mu_mean * y[t-1] * dt) + (gamma_grad * liquidity_grad_t[t-1] * dt) 
                + (gamma_vspri * scalp_index_t[t-1] * dt)+ (ou_drift[t] * dt) + jump[t-1] * y[t-1] * dt;
    
	sd_S[t] =   sigma_s[t]* y[t-1] * sqrt(dt);  
	}
}

model {
   /*  ---- Price diffusion GBM-SV process Priors ---- */ 
  
   // It assumes a weakly informative prior, allowing the asset's long-term trend 
   // to be guided purely by the price sample.
   mu_mean ~ normal(0, 1);
   /*  ---- Price diffusion GBM-SV process Priors ---- */ 

   /*

      Weakly informative prior for the non-linear curvature parameter (ou_shape).
      The gamma(2, 2) distribution shrinks the parameter toward a baseline of 1.0 
      (linear mean reversion) while providing enough probability mass for the sampler 
      to discover sub-linear (concave) or super-linear (convex) dynamics without 
      risking numerical overflow during warm-up.
   */
   ou_shape ~ gamma(2, 2);
   /*
      Weakly informative prior for the EMA distance multiplier (beta).
      Centered at zero to assume no initial structural shift between EMAs, 
      with a standard deviation of 0.5 allowing the data to easily scale 
      the attractor target inward or outward without inducing numerical instability.
   */
   beta ~ normal(0, 0.5);  
   /*
      Weakly informative prior for the base mean-reversion speed.
      The lognormal(0.5, 0.7) distribution bounds phi_reversion strictly above zero, 
      concentrating most probability mass between 0.4 and 4.5 (median ~ 1.65). 
      This accommodates the small scale of absolute price deviations (pips/dollars)
      while preventing extreme initial values that could cause numerical divergence.
   */
   phi_reversion ~ lognormal(0.5, 0.7); 
   /*
      It uses the expected mean of extreme excesses (eva_mean) as the center of the 
      distribution, limiting the variability of this parameter to prevent common 
      daily noise from being erroneously classified as structural jumps.
   */
   mu_j ~ normal(eva_mean, eva_sd * 0.5); 
  
   // Jump variability is guided by the standard deviation of the EVA tail.
   sigma_j ~ normal(eva_sd, eva_sd * 0.2) T[0,]; 
   
   // Standardizes the latent shock increments to a standard normal distribution,
   jump_std ~ normal(0, 1);
       
   // Baseline jump probability "alpha_jump" normal with mean  ~ 0.045
   alpha_jump ~ normal(0.045, 0.01) T[0,1];
   
   /*
      
	  Weakly informative priors for the two absolute scale exogenous coefficients.
      Centered at zero to enforce a neutral baseline, with a standard deviation of 0.001 
      to scale their independent structural impacts within the typical nominal daily 
      price variation (pips/dollars) of foreign exchange markets.
   */
   gamma_grad  ~ normal(0, 0.001);
   gamma_vspri ~ normal(0, 0.001);
   
   // ===========================================================
   // Take-Profit and Stop-Loss Scaling Factors
   // ===========================================================
   k_tp_trend ~ normal(2.0, 0.4) T[0,]; // Ensures a positive and focused TP around 2x the volume.
   k_sl_trend ~ normal(1.2, 0.3) T[0,]; // Maintains a safe technical stop-loss in trending conditions.

   k_tp_scalp ~ normal(0.8, 0.2)  T[0,];   // Short targets for the sideways market
   k_sl_scalp ~ normal(0.6, 0.15) T[0.1,]; // Truncated at 0.1 to never allow a zero stop.
  
   
   // ===========================================================
   // Day-of-Week Effects
   // ===========================================================
   for (i in 1:5){
     beta_dow[i] ~ normal(0, 0.3);
   }   
   // Jump activation coefficients
   beta_breakout ~ normal(1.2, 0.5) T[0,];  // Broke out -> odds ×3.3 (Strictly positive sign)
   beta_near_sr ~ normal(0.8, 0.5) T[0,];   // Compression/Support -> odds ×2.2

   // If beta_ema also follows a similar activation or direction logic:
   beta_ema ~ normal(0.6, 0.5) T[0,];  // Trend alignment
   
   /*  ---- Priors for regimes on Innovations "Eta" ---- */
   mu_1_state[1] ~  normal(-6.0, 0.6); // Low-volatility mean regime
   /*  
         low-vol regime around sd â‰ˆ 0.03â€“0.06 (very calm days)
         Implied median sd = exp(-6/2) = exp(-3) â‰ˆ 0.050
         95% prior sd-range â‰ˆ 0.018 -> 0.14
   */
   mu_1_state[2] ~  normal(-3.8, 0.8); // High-volatility mean regime
   /*
         Implied median sd = exp(-3.8/2) = exp(-1.9) â‰ˆ 0.149
         95% prior sd-range â‰ˆ 0.055 -> 0.40
   */ 
   //   
   /*  ---- HMM Transition Probabilities ----     */
   p_remain ~ beta(20, 1.0);  // High persistence of remaining in a state     
   /* 
           ---- volatility/momentum should evolve smoothly ----
      Encourages the model to learn slow-moving, stable momentum patterns in volatility, 
      which is plausible for financial time series where latent dynamics often evolve gradually.
   */
   //    Centered around 0.9, but openned to be anywhere between 0.8 and 1.0. 
   //    Helping to get persistent temporal dependencies
   momentum ~ normal(0.9, 0.1) T[0,1];  
   epsilon ~ beta(2.0, 2.0);     // "uniform-ish" over [0,1]
   //
   /*  ----  Prices log volatility AR-2 Coefficients Priors ---- */
   rho1 ~  normal(0.90, 0.10) T[-1,1] ; //  High persistence
   rho2 ~  normal(0.00, 0.20) T[-1,1];  // Allows curvature but avoids large oscillations
   // 
   /* ---- Auxiliary Parkinson volatility proxy    
   phi_rv  ~  normal(0.00, 0.10) T[-1,1];
   // ===========================================================
   // Macroeconomic News Effects
   // ===========================================================
   /*
      Regime 1:
      Market efficiently absorbs macroeconomic news.
   */
   beta_usa_state[1] ~ normal(0.002, 0.002);
   beta_eur_state[1] ~ normal(0.0015, 0.002);

   /*
      Regime 2:
      Elevated sensitivity to macroeconomic releases.
   */
   beta_usa_state[2] ~ normal(0.01, 0.005);
   beta_eur_state[2] ~ normal(0.008, 0.005);
  
   // ===========================================================
   // Geopolitical Risk Effects
   // ===========================================================
   gamma_geo_state[1] ~ normal(0.05, 0.05);   // almost irrelevant in calm markets
   gamma_geo_state[2] ~ normal(0.5,  0.2);    // amplifies crises
  
   // Positive volatility-volume link, avoids near-zero issues
   /*   ---- Prior for `tau` (volatility of log-volatility) ----
   
      Lower ,  long-term (low-frequency),  volatility compocurrent.
          Encourages smooth transitions and persistent volatility structure.
   */
   tau_state[1] ~  normal(0.00, 0.25) T[0,];
   /*
        Higher , short-term (high-frequency) ,  log-innovation variance regime.
          Allows for explosive moves in volatility (e.g., crisis, shock).
  
   */
   tau_state[2] ~  normal(0.00, 0.40) T[0,];     
   /*  
         ---- log-volatility for price drift innovation ----
						 
   */
   //   
   n[1] ~ normal(mu_regime[1] , tau_regime[1]*sqrt(1-square(rho)));	   
   n[2] ~ normal(mu_regime[2] , tau_regime[2]*sqrt(1-square(rho)));   
   /* 
      
	  By using z[t-1] and y[t-1] in the mean of n[t] is logically consistent: 
      n[t] depends on previous market activity (y[t-1]) and the accumulated "momentum" 
      from past volatility shocks (z[t-1]).
	  
	  "Stochastic volatility model with leverage" (Model 5, likely based on Wang, 2016)
	  
   */	  
   for (t in 3:N){
     n[t] ~ normal(mu_regime[t] + beta10_regime[t]*h[t-1]+ rho*tau_regime[t]*exp(-0.5*z[t-1])*log(y[t - 1]/y[t - 2]), tau_regime[t]*sqrt(1-
            square(rho)));
   }
   // 
   /*  
            ---- Priors for  MomentumLSTM Scalar Parameters ----
      These are given standard normal priors, typical for neural network weights/biases. 
   */
   // ===========================================================
   // LSTM Scalar Priors for Stochastic Volatility 
   // =========================================================== 
   C_1 ~ normal(0.0,2.00);            // Initial cell state
   h_1 ~ normal(0.0,2.00);            // Initial hidden state   
   thetv_o_scalar ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Output gate momentum vector
   thnw_o_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Output gate weight
   thnb_o_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Output gate bias
   thnv_d_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Cell state candidate momentum vector
   thnw_d_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Cell candidate weight
   thnb_d_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Cell candidate bias
   thnv_i_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Input gate momentum
   thnw_i_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Input gate weight
   thnb_i_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Input gate bias
   thnv_f_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Forget gate momentum
   thnw_f_scalar  ~ normal(0.0,2.0); 
   /*  Forget Gate Bias weight 
          encourages memory retention (sigmoid(1) ~ 0.73)
   */
   thnb_f_scalar  ~ normal(1.0,0.5); 
   //
   for( t in 1:N){
      /* ---- Likehood for price volatility innovations ---- */
      y[t] ~  student_t(nu, mean_S[t], sd_S[t]);
   }
}



generated quantities{
    /*
       Price-Level Dynamic Attractor (Out-of-Sample Projector):
       Computes the target attractor price at the final time index T using the 
       Fast EMA, adjusted by its distance from the Slow EMA via the beta parameter.
    */
    real mu_target_new;

    /*
       Absolute Scale Deviation (Out-of-Sample Projector):
       Measures the final absolute nominal distance (in raw price/pips) between 
       the last observed closing price y[T] and the projected dynamic attractor.
    */
    real deviation_new;
   
    /*
       HMM-Based Modulation (Out-of-Sample Smooth Threshold):
       Extracts the last available regime probability of the consolidation/chop state 
       to compute the smooth activation threshold (ou_gate) for the next step ahead.
    */
    real p_chop_new; 
    real ou_gate_new;

    /*
       Non-Linear Reversion Force (Out-of-Sample Projector):
       Calculates the final elastic correction force. If price is above the attractor, 
       the force pushes downward. The non-linear curvature is determined by ou_shape.
    */
    real sign_deviation_new;
    real ou_reversion_new;

    /*
       Integrated Drift in Brownian Motion (Out-of-Sample Projector):
       Assigns the absolute reversion force directly to the drift vector for the 
       one-step-ahead forecast. No scaling by y[T] is required as units remain nominal.
    */
   real ou_drift_new;
   real theta_eff;
   /*
     
     Estimated Mean Reversion Half-Life (in days/D1 bars).
     Calculates the expected time to reduce a price imbalance by half.
    
      - Short half-life (< 3 days) : Rapid reversion / Sideways market (Range).
      - Long half-life (> 10 days) : Weak pull / Persistent trend.
      - Limit cap (999.0)          : Pure trend (or_gate -> 0), reversion disabled.
  
   */
   real half_life;
   real abs_dev;
   /*  
     Forecasted (Simulated) new physical volatility projected for N+1
	 
   */
   vector [N+1] sigma_s_new;
   /*

   */
   vector [N+1] mean_S_new;

   vector [N+1] beta10_regime_new;

   vector [N+1] n_new;

         after combining:
   
           - HMM regime probabilities
           - Stochastic volatility forecasts
           - Predictive distribution asymmetry

     +1 = Long
      0 = Neutral

   real pa_score_bull = 0.0;

   // ===========================================================
   // Directional Probabilities
   // ===========================================================
   /*
      Posterior probabilities of upward and downward price
      movement relative to the current market level.
  
         p_up   = P(Y_future > Y_current)
         p_down = P(Y_future < Y_current)
   */
   real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0> p_up;
   real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0> p_down;

   // ===========================================================
   // Risk-Reward and Bayesian Trading Edge Metrics
   // ===========================================================
   /*
      reward :
            Expected profit toward the target quantile.
  
      risk :
         Expected adverse excursion measured using
         Expected Shortfall.
  
      rr :
         Classical reward-to-risk ratio.
  
      ERR :
        Probability-weighted Expected Risk-Reward ratio.
  
        ERR > 1 implies that expected reward,
        adjusted by directional probability,
        exceeds expected risk.
  
      edge :
        Net directional advantage:
           edge = p_up - p_down
  
         Positive values favor long exposure.
        Negative values favor short exposure.
   */  
   real reward_long;
   real risk_long; 
   real rr_long;
   real reward_short;  
   real risk_short;
   real rr_short;
   real ERR_long;
   real ERR_short;
   real delta_ERR;
   real edge;

      Copy observed data to new vectors for prediction
   */
   v_g_o_new[1:N] = v_g_o;
   v_n_d_new[1:N] = v_n_d;
   v_g_i_new[1:N] = v_g_i;
   v_g_f_new[1:N] = v_g_f;

   n_d_new[1:N] = n_d;
