           * Rectified Linear Unit (ReLU) activation function: max(0, x)
             Simplely, widely used activation that outputs the input directly if positive,
             otherwise outputs zero.
  */
}

data {
  // Time Series declaration 
  int<lower=1> T;
  vector[T] y;
  int<lower=1> M;          // Candlestick pivot radius for fibonacci calculations
  vector[T] open;          // Opening prices (Required for Price Action Wick Ratios)
  vector[T] high;          // High prices (Required for Price Action Wick Ratios)
  vector[T] low;           // Low prices (Required for Price Action Wick Ratios)
  vector[T] rv;
  int<lower=0,upper=1> date[T, 5];
  // Historical News Dummies (Past)
  vector<lower=0, upper=1> [T] news_usa;      // 1 if USD event, 0 otherwise
  vector<lower=0, upper=1> [T] news_eur;      // 1 if EUR event, 0 otherwise
  vector<lower=0, upper=1> [T] news_geo;      // 1 if geopolitical event, 0 otherwise
  int news_usa_tomorrow;
  int news_eur_tomorrow;
  int news_geo_tomorrow;
  int holidays_usa_tomorrow;
  int holidays_eur_tomorrow;
  // Price action indicators
  vector[T] RSI;
  vector[T] MACD_Line;
  vector[T] MACD_Histogram;
  vector[T] ADX;
  vector[T] ATR;
  vector[T] CVD;
  // Daily Order-Flow Proxy (DOFP) over final model direction probability
  real dofp;   
  vector[T] D_breakout;
  vector[T] D_near_sr;
  vector[T] D_ema_pressure;
  vector[T] liquidity_grad_t;
  vector[T] ema_medium;
  vector[T] ema_fast;
  vector[T] ema_slow;
  vector[T] scalp_index_t;
  vector[T] ema_signal_t;
  vector[T] ema_compression_t;
  // TP & SL levels 
  real farthest_resistance;   
  real farthest_support;
  real big_figure_resistence;
  real big_figure_support; 
  //  Degrees of freedom t-student likelihood
  real nu;
  //  EVA parameters
  real tau_slab; 
  real p_jump;
  real tau_spike; 
  //  Truncation of innovation long memory 
  int J;   
}


parameters {
  /*
  =============================
   Regime-specific parameters
  =============================
  */
  
  // Fractional integration parameter (long memory) by volatility regime
  real<lower= 0.0, upper= 0.5> d[2];
  
  // Day-of-week fixed effects (shared across regimes)
  vector[5] beta_dow;

  // Day-of-week effects on regime transition probabilities
  matrix[2, 5] gamma_dow;

  // Markov transition matrix between regimes (rows sum to 1)
  simplex[2] transition_matrix[2];

  // Process noise (state uncertainty) per regime
  real<lower=0> sigma_proc[2];

  // HAR volatility components (daily, weekly, monthly) by regime
  real<lower=0>  beta_intraday[2];
  real<lower=0>  beta_dailly[2];
  real<lower=0>  beta_weekly[2];
  real<lower=0>  beta_monthly[2];
  
  // Macro news impact on volatility by regime
  real<lower=0>  beta_usa[2];   // US macro events (USD-driven shocks)
  real<lower=0>  beta_eur[2];   // Eurozone macro events (EUR-driven shocks)
  real<lower=0>  gamma_geo[2];  // Geopolitical risk amplification

  // Baseline volatility level (intercept) by regime
  real<lower=0>  intercept_r[2];
          
  /*  
  =======================================================
      Regime-dependent risk-reward scaling parameters
  =======================================================

     These parameters control the magnitude of Take-Profit (TP) and Stop-Loss (SL)
     levels as multiples of tail-based risk measures (VaR / Expected Shortfall),
     conditional on the latent market regime.
     
     - Trend regime: allows larger profit targets and wider stops
     - Scalp regime: enforces tighter targets and faster risk control

     They act as a decision layer on top of the predictive distribution,
     translating probabilistic forecasts into executable trading levels.
     
  */
  real<lower=0> k_tp_trend;  // TP scaling factor in trending regime (captures extended moves)
  real<lower=0> k_tp_scalp;  // TP scaling factor in scalping regime (short-term gains)

  real<lower=0> k_sl_trend;  // SL scaling factor in trending regime (absorbs volatility noise)
  real<lower=0> k_sl_scalp;  // SL scaling factor in scalping regime (tight risk control)
    
    
  /*                 ==== continuous (marginalized) volatility jump ==== 
                              Piironen e Vehtari (2017a, 2017b).           */
							  
  // =============================
  // Jump and microstructure effects
  // =============================
  
  // Spike-and-slab standard deviations (small vs large jumps)
  real<lower=0> sigma_spike;  // near-zero moves (no jump)
  real<lower=0> sigma_slab;   // large jump regime 
    
  // Baseline probability of a jump (logit scale)
  real alpha_jump;

  // Effects on jump probability (logit model)
  real beta_breakout;  // breakout events increase jump likelihood
  real beta_near_sr;   // proximity to support/resistance (compression)
  real beta_ema;       // EMA pressure / trend structure effect

  // Jump impact on volatility (amplification factor)
  real<lower=0> kappa[2];

  // Scaling for jump probability dynamics (optional smoothing / dispersion)
  real<lower=0> sigma_probs;

  // Latent jump process (continuous spike-and-slab)
  vector[T] jump;
    
  // =============================
  // State dynamics coefficients
  // =============================

  // Liquidity impact on price (by regime)
  real<lower=-1,upper=1> beta_liq[2];

  // Jump impact on price dynamics (by regime)
  real<lower=-1,upper=1> beta_jump[2];

  // Volatility impact on latent trend (momentum amplification)
  real beta_vol[2];
  
  // GRU signal weight in price equation (learned momentum)
  real<lower=-1,upper=1> phi_gru[2]; 
  
  // MA(1)-type coefficient for innovation memory (transformed later)
  real<lower=-1,upper=1> theta_raw[2];
  /*  
                        ==== MomentumRNN hyperparameters: ====
     momentum (alpha): Controls inertia of momentum vectors (v_g_o, v_n_d, etc.).
     A value of 0.90 is common in neural networks, smoothing updates.
     Higher values (closer to 1) mean more inertia.
  */ 
  real<lower=0.0,upper=1.0> momentum;
  /*
     epsilon (eta): Controls the influence of Tent volatility innovations (n[t-1])
     on the momentum vectors. Often analogous to a learning rate.
  */
  real<lower=0,upper=1> epsilon;
  /*
             ==== GJR-GARCH Model Parameters  ====
      Omega (constant term): Base var_tiance for each GARCH state.
  */ 
   vector <lower=0> [2] omega; 
  /*
     Alpha (ARCH term): Coefficients for squared past residuals (symmetric response).
  */  
  vector <lower=0> [2] alpha;
  /*
     Psi (Leverage term): Coefficients for the asymmetric effect of negative shocks (Glosten, Jagannathan, Runkle).
     Multiplied by (negative residual)^2 to capture asymmetry.
   
  */
  vector <lower=0> [2] psi;
  /*
     Beta (GARCH term): Coefficients for persistence of past conditional var_tiances.
     'q' here refers to the order of MA in the mean, which seems to be reused for GARCH here. 
  */ 
  vector <lower=0,upper=1> [2] beta;
  //   Initial GARCH variance
  vector <lower=0> [2] sigma0;
  vector <lower=0,upper=1> [2]  phix_residuals;
  /*  
               ==== Scaled GRU Gate Parameters (MomentumRNN-like) ====
      These are scalar versions of the weights for the var_tious gates and cell states,
      multiplied by epsilon and used in the momentum update step. 
  */
  /*
   ===========================================================   
    Forward MomentumGRU scalar parameters (Conditional mean)
   ===========================================================
   */
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Forget/Update gate (input dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Forget/Update gate (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward;   // Bias for forget/update gate
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward;   // Candidate hidden state (hidden state dependent)
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward;   // Bias for candidate hidden state
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  theta_h_gru_scalar_forward;  // Candidate hidden state (input dependent)
   /*
   ===========================================================
    Backward MomentumGRU scalar parameters (Conditional mean)
   ===========================================================
   */
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnv_f_gru_scalar_backward;  // The same here too!!!  
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_f_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_f_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnw_h_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  thnb_h_gru_scalar_backward;   
   real <lower=-1,upper=1>  theta_h_gru_scalar_backward;
   /*        
      ==== GRU initial States Priors ====
   */
   // Initial hidden state for GRUs
   real h_0_forward;
   real h_0_backward;
}

transformed parameters {
  // Initial states
  vector[2] x0;
  
  // Forward probabilities regime
  vector[2] log_alpha[T];
  
  // Marginal log-likelihood
  vector[T] log_likehood;
   
  // Estimated residuals by state at t-1
  vector[2] innovation_prev[T];
   
  // Innovation for state k in t
  real innovation;
  vector[2] innovation_raw[T];

  // Estimated states posterior
  vector[2] x_est[T, 2];
  
  // Regime transition
  vector[2] log_trans_local;
  
  // Estimated states prior
  vector[2] x_p[T, 2];

  // Covariance of process noise (MA(1))
  matrix[2,2] Q;

  // Predicted covariance
  matrix[2,2] P_p[T, 2];
  
  // Local State Transition Jacobian (Regime-Dependent)  
  matrix[2,2] A;

  // Measurement residuals
  vector[2] var_residuals[T];
  vector[2] R[T];
  vector[2] sigma_observed[T];
  
  // Scalar predictive variance of the observation (One value per time and regime)
  matrix[T,2] S;
  
  // Total uncertainty (EKF like + HAR sigma observed)
  real total_uncertainty_prior[T, 2];
  real total_uncertainty_posterior[T, 2];
  
  real <lower=-1,upper=1> theta_ma[2];

  // Kalman gain
  vector[2] K;

  // Jump spike-slab
  vector<lower=0.0, upper=1.0>[T] pi_z;
  
  // Obsezation matrix: maps latent state [price, trend] to obsezed y_t
  // Only price is observed -> y_t = price + noise
  vector[2] H;
  
  // Identity matrix: used in Joseph-form covariance update
  // Ensures numerical stability and positive semi-definiteness of P
  matrix[2,2] I;
  
  // Covariance estimated by regime
  matrix[2,2] P_est[T, 2];
    
  /*   
         ==== Bidirectional GRU momentum vectors ====  
  */
  //  Corresponds to the update/reset gate ***(Forward)    
  vector [T] v_g_f_gru_forward;
  // Corresponds to the update/reset gate  ***(Forward)
  vector [T] v_h_gru_forward;
  //  Corresponds to the update/reset gate ***(Backward)  
  vector [T] v_g_f_gru_backward;
  // Corresponds to the update/reset gate  ***(Backward)  
  vector [T] v_h_gru_backward; 
  /*        
                              ==== GRU States ====
        g_f_gru: Forget/Update gate for the GRU.
        h_gru: Hidden states of the GRU, which will be used in the mean component.
  */
  vector [T] g_f_gru_forward;
  vector [T] h_gru_forward; 
  vector [T] h_gru_forward_updated; 
  vector [T] g_f_gru_backward;
  vector [T] h_gru_backward;
  vector [T] h_gru_backward_updated;
  /*   
                  ==== Concatenated Hidden State from Bidirectional GRU ====
      
	  Combines the forward and backward hidden states, often by averaging or concatenation.
      This combined state summarizes information from both past and future contexts for each time point.
  
  */
  vector [T] h_gru_concatenate; 
  /*   

                  ==== HYPER-PARAMETERS (Hard-coded from empirical priors) ====
				  
      These constants define the behavior of the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) process.
      We use fixed values to stabilize the UKF and reduce parameter identification 
      issues between the latent GRU signals and the trend speed mean-reversion component.

  */
  vector[2] phi;            // Mean-reversion speed: [1] Weak (Trend), [2] Strong (Chop)		
  vector[2] mu_long[T];     // Attractor level: Anchored to global price equilibrium
  vector[2] ou_shape;       // Reversion elasticity: [1] Sub-linear (soft), [2] Super-linear (aggressive)
  vector[2] p_regime_prev;  // Filtered regime probabilities from previous timestep: [1] Trend persistence, [2] Chop persistence
  real p_chop;              // Probability of chop/consolidation regime inferred from previous HMM filtering step
    
  //===============================================
  // Fractional memory weights (long-memory filter)
  //===============================================
  
  // w[k][j] stores fractional differencing weights for regime k
  // These weights approximate a power-law decay, capturing long-range dependence

  vector[J] w[2];
  
  // Based on E[Beta(2, 20)] ~ 0.09 and E[Beta(5, 5)] = 0.50
  phi[1] = 0.09;   // almost no reversal
  phi[2] = 0.50;   // stronger reversal

  // Initial equilibrium points  
  mu_long[1,1] = ema_fast[1];
  mu_long[1,2] = ema_slow[1];

  // Based on Median[lognormal(log(0.5), 0.25)]: 0.5 (Trend-following dampening) and 
  // Median[lognormal(log(1.2), 0.30)]: 1.2 (Chop-regime penalty)
  ou_shape[1] = 0.5;
  ou_shape[2] = 1.2;
  
  for (k in 1:2) {
    // First weight (lag 0) = 1 (identity component)
    w[k][1] = 1;

    // Recursive construction of fractional weights:
    // w_j = w_{j-1} * ((j-1 - d) / j)
    // Produces slowly decaying coefficients when d > 0 (long memory)
	
    for (j in 2:J) {
      w[k][j] = w[k][j-1] * ((j-1 - d[k]) / j);
    }
    /*
    =============================
     Effective MA(1) coefficient
    =============================
    */
	
	// theta_raw is mapped to (-1,1) via tanh to ensure stability
    // Then scaled by (1 - d[k]) to reduce short-memory impact
    // when long-memory (d[k]) is strong
	
    theta_ma[k] = tanh(theta_raw[k]) * (1 - d[k]);
  }   
  // x0[1]: Initial observed price
  x0[1] = y[1]; 
  
  // x0[2]: Initial Trend (Drift)
  x0[2] = y[2] - y[1];  // returns (price speed displacement)  
    
  /*
  =============================
  Jump probability (logistic model)
  =============================
  */ 
  /* 
     pi_z[t] is the probability of a jump at time t.
     It is modeled via a logistic regression combining:
 
         - Price action signals (breakouts, support/resistance proximity, EMA pressure)
         - Calendar effects (day-of-week dummies)
  
    This captures how market conditions and timing influence the likelihood of extreme moves.
  
  */
  for (t in 1:T) {
     pi_z[t] = inv_logit(alpha_jump + beta_breakout * D_breakout[t] + beta_near_sr  * D_near_sr[t] 
	    + beta_ema * D_ema_pressure[t] + beta_dow[1] * date[t,1]
        + beta_dow[2] * date[t,2]
        + beta_dow[3] * date[t,3]
        + beta_dow[4] * date[t,4]
        + beta_dow[5] * date[t,5]);
  }
  //
  var_residuals[1,1] =  ReLU(omega[1] 
				+ beta[1] * square(sigma0[1]));
				
  R[1,1] = intercept_r[1]  + phix_residuals[1]*var_residuals[1,1]
       + beta_usa[1]*news_usa[1]
       + beta_eur[1]*news_eur[1]
       + gamma_geo[1]*news_geo[1];
	   
  var_residuals[1,2] =  ReLU(omega[2] 
				+ beta[2] * square(sigma0[2]));
	  
  R[1,2] = intercept_r[2]  + phix_residuals[2]*var_residuals[1,2]
       + beta_usa[2]*news_usa[1]
       + beta_eur[2]*news_eur[1]
       + gamma_geo[2]*news_geo[1];
	   				

  // HAR variance 
  sigma_observed[1,1] = ReLU(R[1,1] * (1 + kappa[1] * square(jump[1])));
  sigma_observed[1,2] = ReLU(R[1,2] * (1 + kappa[2] * square(jump[1])));
  
  for (k in 1:2) {
      x_p[1, k][1] = x0[1];
      x_p[1, k][2] = x0[2];
	  
      x_est[1, k][1] = x_p[1, k][1];
      x_est[1, k][2] = x_p[1, k][2];
	  
	  innovation_raw[1,k]  = 0.0;
      innovation_prev[1,k] = 0.0;
	  
	  P_p[1,k] = diag_matrix(rep_vector(0.05, 2));
      P_est[1, k] = diag_matrix(rep_vector(0.05, 2));
	  
	  S[1, k] = P_est[1,k][1,1] + sigma_observed[1,k]; 
	  
	  total_uncertainty_prior[1,k]  =    P_p[1,k][1,1] + sigma_observed[1,k];	  
	  total_uncertainty_posterior[1,k] = P_est[1,k][1,1] + sigma_observed[1,k];
	  
	  log_alpha[1, k] = log(0.5) + student_t_lpdf(y[1] | nu,  x_p[1, k][1], sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_prior[1, k])));
	}
   	/*
        ==== Conditional mean. Initialize Bidirectional GRU States and Momentum for t=1 ====
                Sets initial hidden and cell states to zero.
           Sets initial forward and backward momentum  to zero.
    */ 
    // Forward terms   
    // Forward terms   
    v_g_f_gru_forward[1] = 0.0;
    v_h_gru_forward[1] =   0.0;
    v_g_f_gru_forward[1] =  (momentum * v_g_f_gru_forward[1] + epsilon*thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward*h_0_forward);
    g_f_gru_forward[1]   =  inv_logit(v_g_f_gru_forward[1] + thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward*y[1] +  thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward);
    v_h_gru_forward[1]   =  (momentum *v_h_gru_forward[1] + epsilon*thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward*(g_f_gru_forward[1] + h_0_forward));
    h_gru_forward[1]     =   tanh(v_h_gru_forward[1] + theta_h_gru_scalar_forward*y[1]  + thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward);
    h_gru_forward_updated[1] = (1- g_f_gru_forward[1])*h_0_forward +  g_f_gru_forward[1] *h_gru_forward[1];
	// Backward terms 
    v_g_f_gru_backward[1] = 0.0;
    v_h_gru_backward[1] =   0.0;
    v_g_f_gru_backward[1] =  (momentum * v_g_f_gru_backward[1] + epsilon*thnw_f_gru_scalar_backward*h_0_backward);
    g_f_gru_backward[1]   =  inv_logit(v_g_f_gru_backward[1] + thnv_f_gru_scalar_backward*y[T] +  thnb_f_gru_scalar_backward);
    v_h_gru_backward[1]   =  (momentum *v_h_gru_backward[1] + epsilon*thnw_h_gru_scalar_backward*(g_f_gru_backward[1] + h_0_backward));
    h_gru_backward[1]     =  tanh(v_h_gru_backward[1] + theta_h_gru_scalar_backward*y[T]  + thnb_h_gru_scalar_backward);	
    h_gru_backward_updated[1] = (1- g_f_gru_backward[1])*h_0_backward +  g_f_gru_backward[1] *h_gru_backward[1];  
	/*
        GRU update rule: (1 - update_gate) * previous_hidden_state + update_gate * candidate_hidden_state
        Here, "g_f_gru_forward" and "g_f_gru_backward" are acting as the update gate.
    */
    //
    /*
                   ==== Concatenate Bidirectional GRU Hidden States (for t = 1) ====
                  Combines the forward hidden state  with the backward hidden state at time 't'
     	  
    */ 
   h_gru_concatenate[1] =  (h_gru_forward_updated[1] + h_gru_backward_updated[1])/2; 
   //
   H = [1.0, 0.0]';
   I = diag_matrix(rep_vector(1.0, 2));
   //
   for (t in 2:T) {
       /* 
                             ==== Main Time Series Loop (t=2 to T) ====

	                ==== Forward and Backward MomentumGRU Update within the loop ====
          Computes momentum, gate, and hidden state for GRU at each time step,
          using the previous hidden state (h_gru_forward[t-1]) and previous returns obs (y[t-1]).
	
	  */
       /* 
                             ==== Main Time Series Loop (t=2 to T) ====

	                ==== Forward and Backward MomentumGRU Update within the loop ====
          Computes momentum, gate, and hidden state for GRU at each time step,
          using the previous hidden state (h_gru_forward[t-1]) and previous returns obs (y[t-1]).
	
	  */
	  v_g_f_gru_forward[t] = (momentum * v_g_f_gru_forward[t-1] + epsilon*thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward*h_gru_forward[t-1]);
	  g_f_gru_forward[t] = inv_logit(v_g_f_gru_forward[t] + thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward*y[t-1] + thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward);
	  v_h_gru_forward[t] =  (momentum *v_h_gru_forward[t-1] + epsilon*thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward*(g_f_gru_forward[t] + h_gru_forward[t-1]));
	  h_gru_forward[t] =  tanh(v_h_gru_forward[t] + theta_h_gru_scalar_forward*y[t-1] + thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward);
	  h_gru_forward_updated[t] = (1- g_f_gru_forward[t])*h_gru_forward[t-1] +  g_f_gru_forward[t] *h_gru_forward[t]; 
	  /*            
                	==== backward MomentumGRU Update within the loop ====
          Computes momentum, gate, and hidden state back GRU at each time step,
          using the previous hidden state (h_gru_backward[t-1]) and previous inverted returns obs (y[T-t]).
    
	  */
	  v_g_f_gru_backward[t] = (momentum * v_g_f_gru_backward[t-1] + epsilon*thnw_f_gru_scalar_backward*h_gru_backward[t-1]);
      g_f_gru_backward[t] = inv_logit(v_g_f_gru_backward[t] + thnv_f_gru_scalar_backward*y[max(T-t+1,1)] + thnb_f_gru_scalar_backward);
      v_h_gru_backward[t] =  (momentum *v_h_gru_backward[t-1] + epsilon*thnw_h_gru_scalar_backward*(g_f_gru_backward[t] + h_gru_backward[t-1]));
      h_gru_backward[t] =  tanh(v_h_gru_backward[t] + theta_h_gru_scalar_backward*y[max(T-t+1,1)] + thnb_h_gru_scalar_backward);
      h_gru_backward_updated[t] = (1- g_f_gru_backward[t])*h_gru_backward[t-1] +  g_f_gru_backward[t] *h_gru_backward[t];  	
      /*
                   ==== Concatenate Bidirectional GRU Hidden States (for t > 1) ====
                  Combines the forward hidden state  with the backward hidden state at time 't'
     	  
      */
      h_gru_concatenate[t] =  (h_gru_forward_updated[t] + h_gru_backward_updated[t])/2; 
      //
	  p_regime_prev = softmax(log_alpha[t-1]);
      p_chop = p_regime_prev[2];   // regime 1 = trend, regime 2 = chop
	  //
      for (k in 1:2) {
        // Prediction Step
		
        mu_long[t,1] = ema_fast[t];
        mu_long[t,2] = ema_slow[t];
				
		if (k == 1){
          x_p[t, 1] = predict_ukf(x_est[t-1, 1],P_est[t-1, 1],  phi_gru[1], h_gru_concatenate[t],theta_ma[1], phi[1] ,mu_long[t-1,1], 
							ou_shape[1], 1.0 - p_chop, beta_vol[1], innovation_prev[t-1,1], 
							liquidity_grad_t[t-1],beta_liq[1] ,beta_jump[1],D_breakout[t-1],
							scalp_index_t[t-1],ema_signal_t[t-1],ema_compression_t[t-1], sigma_observed[t-1,1]); 
		} else {
		  x_p[t, 2] = predict_ukf(x_est[t-1, 2],P_est[t-1, 2],  phi_gru[2], h_gru_concatenate[t],theta_ma[2],  phi[2], mu_long[t-1,2], 
							ou_shape[2], p_chop, beta_vol[2], innovation_prev[t-1,2],
							liquidity_grad_t[t-1],beta_liq[2], beta_jump[2],D_breakout[t-1],
							scalp_index_t[t-1],ema_signal_t[t-1], ema_compression_t[t-1], sigma_observed[t-1,2]); 
		}
		
        Q = diag_matrix(rep_vector(square(sigma_proc[k]), 2));
		
		// 2) Tent innovation of K regime
		innovation = y[t] - x_p[t, k][1];
        innovation_raw[t,k] = innovation;
        // fractional convolution filter of innovations (Long-memory)
        innovation_prev[t,k] = innovation_raw[t,k];
        for (j in 1:J) {
          if (t - j >= 1)
            innovation_prev[t,k] += w[k][j] * innovation_raw[t-j,k];
        }
		
		/*
		
		************************************************************
		
		Disclaimer : 
		
          An alternative implementation was initially considered in
          which the UKF prediction function would propagate both the 
		  state mean and its covariance using the Unscented Transform,
          reconstructing x_pred and P_pred directly from the propagated
          sigma points.

          However, Stan functions can return only a single object and
          do not support output (reference) arguments as in C/C++.
          Consequently, a function such as:

               void predict_ukf(..., vector x_pred, matrix P_pred)

          cannot simultaneously update both the predicted state and
          covariance outside its local scope.

          Although this limitation could be circumvented by splitting
          the implementation into multiple functions or by packing
          multiple outputs into a single object, doing so would
          considerably increase code complexity, duplicate sigma-point
          propagation logic, and reduce code maintainability.

          Therefore, a hybrid prediction strategy is adopted.The latent state mean 
		  is propagated through the full nonlinear Unscented Transform, preserving 
		  the main advantage of the UKF in capturing nonlinear state dynamics.The state 
		  covariance, however, is propagated separately using the classical local linear
		  approximation:
    
              P_pred = A * P * A' + Q

          where A is a regime-dependent local transition matrix and Q
          is the process noise covariance.

          This approach retains the nonlinear prediction of the latent
          state while keeping covariance propagation computationally
          efficient, numerically stable, and fully compatible with
          Stan's function interface.
      
          Summerizing, this results in a hybrid UKF/EKF formulation:
          nonlinear sigma-point propagation for the state prediction,combined with an 
		  EKF-style covariance update based on a local linearization of the system dynamics.

		************************************************************
		
        ===========================================================
        Local State Transition Jacobian (Regime-Dependent)
        ===========================================================
				
        This matrix controls how uncertainty propagates through the
        latent state dynamics during the UKF prediction step.

        State vector:
            x = [price, trend]

        ================
        Interpretation:
        ================
		
        A[1,1] : Persistence of the price level
        A[1,2] : Influence of trend on future price
        A[2,1] : Feedback from price into trend
        A[2,2] : Persistence of the latent trend state

        The matrix is made regime-dependent so that covariance
        evolves differently across market conditions.

        ============================================================
        REGIME 1 : Trending / Persistent Market
        ============================================================

        Characteristics:
		
        - Strong directional persistence
        - Price continues moving in the trend direction
        - Momentum survives longer
        - Lower mean reversion
        - Smoother trajectories

        Covariance behavior:
        - Uncertainty propagates aggressively forward
        - Trend shocks persist through time
        - Price uncertainty accumulates faster

        Interpretation of coefficients:

        A[1,1] = 1.0
            -> Price behaves approximately like a random walk.

        A[1,2] = 1.2
            -> Trend has strong influence on future price movement.

        A[2,2] = 0.95
            -> Latent trend is highly persistent.

        ============================================================
        REGIME 2 : Chop / Mean-Reverting / Scalp Market
        ============================================================

        Characteristics:
		
        - Sideways market behavior
        - Fast reversal of local momentum
        - Microstructure noise dominates
        - Weak directional persistence
        - High local instability but short memory

        Covariance behavior:
		
        - Uncertainty contracts quickly
        - Trend shocks decay rapidly
        - Filter becomes more adaptive and reactive

        Interpretation of coefficients:

        A[1,1] = 0.8
            -> Price persistence is weaker due to local reversals.

        A[1,2] = 0.3
            -> Trend contributes less to future price dynamics.

        A[2,2] = 0.25
            -> Trend memory decays quickly.

        Using a regime-dependent transition matrix allows the UKF
        covariance to adapt to different market structures:

        - Trending regimes propagate uncertainty forward.
        - Chop regimes suppress long-horizon uncertainty.

        This produces more realistic uncertainty dynamics than a
        single fixed transition matrix.

        The matrix acts as a local linearization of the nonlinear
        state dynamics and improves covariance transport compared to:

            P_p = P + Q

        by introducing structured state propagation:

            P_p = A * P * A' + Q

        which better captures:
		
        - trend persistence,
        - regime memory,
        - nonlinear filtering behavior,
        - and market-state-dependent uncertainty.

        ===========================================================
        */
		
		if (k == 1){

		   // Trending regime
   		   A[1,1] = 1.0;
           A[1,2] = 1.2;

           A[2,1] = 0.0;
           A[2,2] = 0.95;

        } else {

          // Chop / mean-reverting regime
          A[1,1] = 0.8;
          A[1,2] = 0.3;

          A[2,1] = 0.0;
          A[2,2] = 0.25;
        }

        // Covariance propagation
        P_p[t, k] = A * P_est[t-1,k] * A' + Q;
		
	    //  3) Baseline GJR-GARCH structure
        var_residuals[t,k] = ReLU(omega[k] + alpha[k]  * square(innovation_prev[t-1,k])
                    + beta[k]   * var_residuals[t - 1,k]);
		if (innovation_prev[t-1,k] < 0){
            var_residuals[t,k] += ReLU(psi[k] * square(innovation_prev[t-1,k]));
	    }       
        // HAR variance  
		if (t-5 <= 0){
            R[t,k] = intercept_r[k]  +  beta_dailly[k]*rv[t-1] + phix_residuals[k]*var_residuals[t-1,k] +
		           beta_usa[k]*news_usa[t] +  beta_eur[k] * news_eur[t] + gamma_geo[k] * news_geo[t];		
		} 
		else if ((t-5 > 0) && (t-22<= 0)){
		    R[t,k] = intercept_r[k] +  beta_dailly[k]*rv[t-1] +  phix_residuals[k]*var_residuals[t-1,k] +
			         + beta_weekly[k] * rv[t-5] + beta_usa[k]*news_usa[t] +  beta_eur[k] * news_eur[t] + gamma_geo[k] * news_geo[t];              		
        }
		else if  ((t-5 > 0) && (t-22 > 0)){
            R[t,k] = intercept_r[k] + beta_dailly[k]*rv[t-1] + phix_residuals[k]*var_residuals[t-1,k] +
			         beta_weekly[k] * rv[t-5] +  beta_monthly[k] * rv[t-22] + beta_usa[k]*news_usa[t] +  beta_eur[k] * news_eur[t] + gamma_geo[k] * news_geo[t]; 	
		}
        sigma_observed[t,k] = ReLU(R[t,k] * (1 + kappa[k] * square(jump[t])));	
			
		S[t, k] = P_p[t, k][1, 1] + sigma_observed[t,k];  
        
        // Kalman Gain by states
        K[1] = P_p[t, k][1, 1] / S[t, k];
        K[2] = P_p[t, k][2, 1] / S[t, k];
				
        // Update
        x_est[t, k] = x_p[t, k] + K * innovation_raw[t,k];	
        P_est[t, k] = (I - K * H') * P_p[t, k] * (I - K * H')' + K * sigma_observed[t,k] * K';
		
		total_uncertainty_prior[t,k]  =    P_p[t, k][1,1] + sigma_observed[t,k];
		total_uncertainty_posterior[t,k] = P_est[t,k][1,1] + sigma_observed[t,k];
                
		// HMM Forward
        for (prev_k in 1:2)
          log_trans_local[prev_k] = log_alpha[t-1, prev_k] + log(transition_matrix[prev_k, k])  
                                    + gamma_dow[k,1] * date[t,1]
                                    + gamma_dow[k,2] * date[t,2]
                                    + gamma_dow[k,3] * date[t,3]
                                    + gamma_dow[k,4] * date[t,4]
                                    + gamma_dow[k,5] * date[t,5]; 
        // likehood (emission)									
        log_alpha[t, k] = log_sum_exp(log_trans_local) + student_t_lpdf(y[t] | nu,  x_p[t, k][1], sqrt(total_uncertainty_prior[t, k]));
	  }
	// marginal log-likelihood
	log_likehood[t] = log_sum_exp(log_alpha[t]);
   }
}

model {

  /*
      ==== Priors for Momentum-GRU Scalar Parameters ====

      Standard Gaussian priors assigned to neural-network-like
      weights and biases governing the Momentum-GRU dynamics.
  */

  // ===========================================================
  // Bidirectional Momentum-GRU Priors (Forward Pass)
  // ===========================================================

  h_0_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.00);                 // Prior for initial hidden state
  theta_h_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);   // Hidden-state transformation coefficient
  thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);    // Forward momentum vector coefficient
  thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);    // Forward update gate weight

  /*
      ==== Forward update gate bias ====

      Slightly positive prior encourages memory retention:
      sigmoid(1) ~ 0.73
  */

  thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(1.0,0.5);    // Forward update gate bias
  thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);    // Hidden-state update weight
  thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);    // Hidden-state update bias


  // ===========================================================
  // Bidirectional Momentum-GRU Priors (Backward Pass)
  // ===========================================================

  h_0_backward ~ normal(0.0,2.00);
  theta_h_gru_scalar_backward ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Hidden-state transformation coefficient
  thnv_f_gru_scalar_backward  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Backward momentum vector coefficient
  thnw_f_gru_scalar_backward  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Backward update gate weight
  thnb_f_gru_scalar_backward  ~ normal(1.0,0.5);  // Backward update gate bias
  thnw_h_gru_scalar_backward  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Hidden-state update weight
  thnb_h_gru_scalar_backward  ~ normal(0.0,2.0);  // Hidden-state update bias
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Markov-Switching State Transition Parameters
  // ===========================================================

  /*
      Market regimes remain persistent in FX dynamics, but with 
      higher uncertainty and more frequent expected switching.

      Prior expected transition matrix:

          [0.71  0.29]
          [0.29  0.71]

      Lower concentration allows the data to easily override 
      this prior if frequent regime changes are observed.
  */

  transition_matrix[1] ~ dirichlet(to_vector({5, 2}));
  transition_matrix[2] ~ dirichlet(to_vector({2, 5}));

  // Regime-dependent volatility sensitivity
  beta_vol[1] ~ normal(0.15, 0.08);
  beta_vol[2] ~ normal(0.50, 0.15);
  
  // GRU influence on latent state dynamics
  phi_gru[1] ~ normal(0.0, 0.5) T[-1,1];
  phi_gru[2] ~ normal(0.0, 0.8) T[-1,1];

  theta_raw[1] ~ normal(0.0, 0.5) T[-1,1];
  theta_raw[2] ~ normal(0.0, 0.8) T[-1,1];
  
  // Liquidity sensitivity coefficients
  // Captures fine-grained microstructure effects on price formation

  beta_liq[1] ~ normal(0.0,0.5);
  beta_liq[2] ~ normal(0.3,0.3);
      
  // Breakout jump amplitudes
  beta_jump[1] ~ normal(0.00,0.03); // Calm regime 
  beta_jump[2] ~ normal(0.00,0.05); // Volatile regime 

  // Measurement noise scaling parameters
  kappa[1] ~ normal(0.6, 0.2);
  kappa[2] ~ normal(1.5, 0.5);

  // Initial state uncertainty
  sigma0[1] ~ normal(0.015, 0.005)  T[0,];
  sigma0[2] ~ normal(0.050, 0.010)  T[0,];

  // Residual persistence
  phix_residuals[1] ~ normal(0.2, 0.1) T[0,1];
  phix_residuals[2] ~ normal(0.5, 0.2) T[0,1];
  /*
      ==== Regime-Specific GJR-GARCH Priors ====

      Omega represents the baseline conditional variance level.
  */

  omega[1] ~ normal(0.005, 0.002) T[0,];
  omega[2] ~ normal(0.03, 0.005) T[0,];
  /*
      Alpha (ARCH effect)

      Measures sensitivity of Tent volatility
      to recent squared innovations.

      Higher values imply stronger volatility reactions
      to recent market shocks.
  */

  alpha[1] ~ normal(0.03, 0.01) T[0,];
  alpha[2] ~ normal(0.10, 0.03) T[0,];
  /*
      Psi (GJR leverage coefficient)

      Captures asymmetry between positive and negative shocks.

      Negative innovations contribute more strongly
      to future volatility than positive innovations
      of equal magnitude.
  */

  psi[1] ~ normal(0.01, 0.005) T[0,];
  psi[2] ~ normal(0.12, 0.03) T[0,];
  /*
      Beta (GARCH persistence)

      Controls persistence of conditional variance.

      Regime 1:
      Highly persistent volatility process.

      Regime 2:
      Faster adaptation to new information.
  */

  beta[1] ~ beta(25, 2);
  beta[2] ~ beta(5, 5);
  /*
      ==== Latent Momentum Persistence ====

      Encourages smooth and slowly evolving latent momentum dynamics,
      consistent with persistent temporal dependencies frequently
      observed in financial markets.
  */

  momentum ~ normal(0.9, 0.05) T[0,1];
  epsilon ~ beta(2, 2);  // Approximately uniform over [0,1]


  // ===========================================================
  // Day-of-Week Effects
  // ===========================================================

  for (i in 1:5){

    beta_dow[i] ~ normal(0, 0.3);

    gamma_dow[1,i] ~ normal(0, 0.05);
    gamma_dow[2,i] ~ normal(0, 0.20);
  }


  // ===========================================================
  // State Process Noise
  // ===========================================================

  sigma_proc[1] ~ normal(0.002, 0.001); // Calm regime
  sigma_proc[2] ~ normal(0.010, 0.003); // Turbulent regime

  // ===========================================================
  // HAR Volatility Structure
  // ===========================================================

  /*
                      ==== HAR Regime 1 (Calm / Trendy) ==== 
       A typical and stable structure where the long term (month) anchors the calm regime.
  */
  beta_dailly[1] ~ normal(0.3, 0.2);
  beta_weekly[1] ~ normal(0.4, 0.2);
  beta_monthly[1] ~ normal(0.6, 0.2);  // more persistent    
  /*
                   ==== HAR Regime 2 (Volatile / Scalping / Choppy) ==== 
       In panics or high volatility, the very short term (daily) completely dominates.
  */
  beta_dailly[2] ~ normal(0.8, 0.3);
  beta_weekly[2] ~ normal(0.6, 0.3);
  beta_monthly[2] ~ normal(0.3, 0.3);  // less persistence
  // ===========================================================
  // Macroeconomic News Effects
  // ===========================================================
  /*
      Regime 1:
      Market efficiently absorbs macroeconomic news.
  */
  beta_usa[1] ~ normal(0.002, 0.002);
  beta_eur[1] ~ normal(0.0015, 0.002);

  /*
      Regime 2:
      Elevated sensitivity to macroeconomic releases.
  */
  beta_usa[2] ~ normal(0.01, 0.005);
  beta_eur[2] ~ normal(0.008, 0.005);
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Geopolitical Risk Effects
  // ===========================================================
  gamma_geo[1] ~ normal(0.05, 0.05);   // almost irrelevant in calm markets
  gamma_geo[2] ~ normal(0.5,  0.2);    // amplifies crises
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Regime-Specific Volatility Intercepts
  // ===========================================================
  intercept_r[1] ~ exponential(20);  // (1/20) = 0.005 (Very low and controlled base volume for a clean trend regime)
  intercept_r[2] ~ exponential(5);   // (1/5)  = 0.2   (Higher base volume for noise/scalping regime)
    
  // ===========================================================
  // Fractional Long-Memory Parameters
  // ===========================================================

  d[1] ~ beta(2,6); // Weak long memory
  d[2] ~ beta(3,4); // Stronger persistence
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Take-Profit and Stop-Loss Scaling Factors
  // ===========================================================
  k_tp_trend ~ normal(2.0, 0.4) T[0,]; // Ensures a positive and focused TP around 2x the volume.
  k_sl_trend ~ normal(1.2, 0.3) T[0,]; // Maintains a safe technical stop-loss in trending conditions.

  k_tp_scalp ~ normal(0.8, 0.2)  T[0,];   // Short targets for the sideways market
  k_sl_scalp ~ normal(0.6, 0.15) T[0.1,]; // Truncated at 0.1 to never allow a zero stop.

  /*
           ==== Continuous Spike-and-Slab Jump Model ====
      Informed priors according to Extreme Value Analysis (EVA)    
  */
  
  // ====- Jump scale (EVA-informed) ==== 
  sigma_spike ~ normal(tau_spike, 0.10);
  sigma_slab  ~ normal(tau_slab, 0.50);
  
  // ====- Jump probability ====-
  alpha_jump ~ normal(p_jump, 0.01) T[0,1];  //baseline jump probability
   
  // Jump activation coefficients
  beta_breakout ~ normal(1.2, 0.5) T[0,];  // Broke out -> odds ×3.3 (Strictly positive sign)
  beta_near_sr ~ normal(0.8, 0.5) T[0,];   // Compression/Support -> odds ×2.2

  // If beta_ema also follows a similar activation or direction logic:
  beta_ema ~ normal(0.6, 0.5) T[0,];  // Trend alignment

  /*
      Marginalized zero-mean continuous spike-and-slab prior
      whose variance is governed by the latent jump probability.
  */

  for (t in 1:T){
    target += log_mix(
      pi_z[t],
      normal_lpdf(jump[t] | 0, sigma_slab),
      normal_lpdf(jump[t] | 0, sigma_spike)
    );
  }

  // ===========================================================
  // Hidden Markov Model Likelihood
  // ===========================================================

  target += log_sum_exp(log_alpha[T]);

}  



generated quantities {
  // ===========================================================
  // Predicted State Covariances
  // ===========================================================
  /*
          One-step-ahead state covariance matrices produced by the
          UKF prediction step before measurement assimilation.
  */
  matrix[2,2] P_p_new_regime1;
  matrix[2,2] P_p_new_regime2;
  
  // Posterior updated Covariance 
  
  matrix[2,2] P_post_new_regime1;
  matrix[2,2] P_post_new_regime2;
  // ===========================================================
  // Innovation Covariances
  // ===========================================================
  /*
          Observation-space covariance matrices used to construct
          the Kalman gain and evaluate predictive uncertainty.
  */
  matrix[2,2] A_new_regime1;
  matrix[2,2] A_new_regime2;
  real S_new_regime1;
  real S_new_regime2;
  real total_uncertainty_prior_new[T+1, 2];  
  real total_uncertainty_posterior_new[T+1, 2];
  // ===========================================================
  // Kalman Gains
  // ===========================================================
  /*
         Adaptive weights determining how strongly incoming
         observations influence latent state corrections.
  */
  vector[2] K_regime1;
  vector[2] K_regime2;  
  // ===========================================================
  // Predictive Innovations
  // ===========================================================
  /*
  
       Simulated observation innovations used to generate
       one-step-ahead state updates under each regime.
  
  */
  real innovation_regime1;
  real innovation_regime2;
  // ===========================================================
  // Hidden Markov Model Forecasts
  // ===========================================================
  /*
        Posterior regime probabilities and one-step-ahead
        transition forecasts.
  */
  matrix[T, 2] regime_probs;
  matrix[T+1, 2] prob_next;
  real p_chop_last;
  // ===========================================================  
  // One-Step-Ahead Latent States
  // ===========================================================
  /*
     Predicted latent state vectors:
   
      x[1] = latent price level
      x[2] = latent directional trend
  */  
  vector[2] x_next_regime1;
  vector[2] x_next_regime2;
  
    // Posterior filtered states after regime simualted update
  vector[2] x_est_final_new[2];
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Predictive Observation Distribution
  // ===========================================================
  /*
        Simulated EURUSD trajectory together with posterior
        predictive mean and volatility estimates.
  */
  vector[T+1] y_sim;
  vector[2] R_new[T+1];
  vector[2] sigma_observed_new[T+1];
  vector[T+1] mu;
  vector[T+1] sigma;
  // ===========================================================
  // OU Process Half-Life
  // =========================================================== 
  /*
  
     Effective Speed ​​of the Mean-Reversion Process (Ornstein-Uhlenbeck).
     Instantaneous rate of attraction toward the equilibrium level,
     dynamically modulated by the UKF/HMM consolidation regime (ou_gate * phi).
  
  */
  real theta_eff;
  /*
     
     Estimated Mean Reversion Half-Life (in days/D1 bars).
     Calculates the expected time to reduce a price imbalance by half.
    
      - Short half-life (< 3 days) : Rapid reversion / Sideways market (Range).
      - Long half-life (> 10 days) : Weak pull / Persistent trend.
      - Limit cap (999.0)          : Pure trend (or_gate -> 0), reversion disabled.
  
  */
  real half_life;
  // ===========================================================
  // Jump Forecast
  // ===========================================================
  /*
       One-step-ahead expected jump realization from the
       spike-and-slab jump process.
  */
  real jumps_new;
  int regime_in_sample;
  // ===========================================================
  // Predictive Log-Likelihood Components (for WAIC / PSIS-LOO)
  // ===========================================================

  vector[2] log_mix_density;   // Conditional log-likelihood under each regime
  vector[2] log_prior;       // Log prior regime probabilities
  
  // log-likehood per regime
  vector[T] log_lik;
  
  // Predicted momentum vectors for GRU 
  real v_g_f_gru_forward_new;
  real v_h_gru_forward_new;  
  real g_f_gru_forward_new;
  real h_gru_forward_new;
  real h_gru_forward_new_updated;
  vector[T+1] h_gru_concatenate_new;
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Trading Signal and Regime Allocation
  // ===========================================================
  /*
         These quantities summarize the model's directional view
         after combining:
   
           - HMM regime probabilities
           - UKF latent trend estimates
           - Stochastic volatility forecasts
           - Predictive distribution asymmetry
   
  signal:
     +1 = Long
      0 = Neutral
     -1 = Short
  */
  real gain;
  real loss;
  int signal;
  // ===========================================================
  // Dynamic Risk Management Parameters
  // ===========================================================
  /*
       Regime-adaptive Take-Profit (TP) and Stop-Loss (SL)
       multipliers.
    
     Trend regime:
       
        Wider profit targets and looser stops.
   
     Scalping / Chop regime:
      
        Tighter exits and smaller risk budgets.
  */  
  real  <lower = 0.0> k_tp;
  real  <lower = 0.0> k_sl;
  real  <lower = 0.0, upper = 1.0> prob_trend;
  real  <lower = 0.0, upper = 1.0> prob_scalp;
  real tp_price;
  real sl_price;
  // ===========================================================
  // Predictive Tail Asymmetry
  // ===========================================================
  /*
  
        Measures the relative strength of upper and lower tails
        in the predictive Student-t distribution.
   
        Large values indicate directional skewness and potential
        asymmetry in future price excursions.
  */
  real <lower = 0.0> tail_ratio_up;
  real <lower = 0.0> tail_ratio_down;
  
  // ===========================================================
  // Predictive Quantiles and Expected Shortfall
  // ===========================================================   
  real q_std_low;
  real q_std_high;  
  real q_low;    // q_low  : downside quantile
  real q_high;   // q_high : upside quantile  
  real ES_up;    // ES_up   : expected upside tail excursion
  real ES_down;  // ES_down : expected downside tail loss
  
  // ==========================================
  //     Price Action Features Integration
  // ==========================================
   
  // 1. BASE DATA AND DIMENTIONS FOR THE TENT CANDLE (N) AND PREVIOUS ONES
  
  real candle_range_N;
  real body_N;
  real body_N1;
  real body_N2;

  real range_N1;
  real upper_wick_N;
  real lower_wick_N;

  real local_vol; 
   
  // ============================================================================
  // 2. WICK MENRICS AND NORMALIZED MOMENTUM
  // ============================================================================
   
  real lower_wick_ratio;
  real upper_wick_ratio;

  real candle_body_norm;
  real prev_candle_body_norm;
  real pa_reversal_momentum;

  // ============================================================================
  // 3. PRICE ACTION STRUCTURES - BULLISH
  // ============================================================================

  // Morning Star Pattern   
  int is_morning_star;
   
  // Bullish Engulfing
  int is_bullish_engulfing;
  
  // Bullish Change of Character (CHOCH)
  real last_swing_high;
  int is_choch_bullish;
   
  // Hammer (Pinbar Bottom)
  int is_hammer;

  // Bullish Break of Structure (BOS)   
  real max_high_5;
  int is_bos_bullish;

  // Bullish Liquidity Sweep (Low Rejection)   
  real min_low_5;
  int is_liquidity_sweep_bullish;

  // Bullish Order Block
  real body_N1_norm;
  int is_bullish_order_block;

  // Bullish Fair Value Gap (FVG)
  real fvg_bullish_size;
  real fvg_bullish_norm;

  // Piercing Line
  int is_piercing_line;

  // Selling Climax (Exhaustion)
  real body_N_norm;
  int is_selling_climax;

  // ============================================================================
  // 4. PRICE ACTION STRUCTURES - BEARISH
  // ============================================================================
   
  // Evening Star Pattern
  int is_evening_star;

  // Shooting Star (Upper Wick RejecNion)
  int is_shooting_star;

  // Bearish Change of Character (CHOCH)
  real last_swing_low;
  int is_choch_bearish;

  // Bearish Breaker Block
  int is_bearish_breaker;

  // Bearish Fair Value Gap (FVG)
  real fvg_bearish_size;
  real fvg_bearish_norm;

  // Buying Climax (ExhausNion)
  int is_buying_climax;

  // ============================================================================
  // 5. NEUTRAL STRUCTURES / LIQUIDITY CONTEXT
  // ============================================================================

  int is_inside_bar;
  int is_nr4;

  real eq_threshold;
  int is_equal_highs;
  int is_equal_lows;
   
  // ============================================================================
  // 6. DOJI & SPINNING TOP VARIATIONS (CANDLE GEOMETRY)
  // ============================================================================
   
  real body_ratio_T;   
  // Standard Doji (Tiny body ≤ 5% of the total range)
  int is_doji;
   
  // Dragonfly Doji (Bottom Rejection / Bullish: No upper wick, long lower wick)
  int is_dragonfly_doji;
   
  // Gravestone Doji (Top Rejection / Bearish: No lower wick, long upper wick.)
  int is_gravestone_doji;
   
  // Long-Legged Doji (Extreme indecision with long wicks on both sides.)
  int is_long_legged_doji;

  // Spinning Top (Spinning Top / Indecision: Small body between 5% and 20%)
  int is_spinning_top;
  
  // Candle showing strong buying momentum, with a dominant body and no or minimal wicks.
  int is_bullish_marubozu;
  
  // Candle showing strong selling momentum, with a dominant body and no or minimal wicks.
  int is_bearish_marubozu;
  
  
  // ============================================================================
  // 7.  ADVANCED SMART MONEY CONCEPTS (SMC) & LIQUIDITY FRAMEWORK
  // ============================================================================

  // Change of Character (CHOCH) - Premature Structural Change
  real swing_high_10;
  real swing_low_10;

  // Break of Structure (BOS) - Trend Continuation
  real swing_high_5;
  real swing_low_5;

  // Liquidity Sweeps (Liquidity Sweep at Highs/Lows – False Breakouts)
  int is_sweep_highs; // Sales Sweep (Bearish)

  int is_sweep_lows; // Buy Sweep (Bullish)

  // Mitigated Order Blocks (OB) at T
  // Bullish OB: Last bearish candle before the strong bullish move that retests support
  int is_bullish_ob;

  // OB Seller: Last bullish candle before the sharp drop that retests resistance
  int is_bearish_ob;
  
  // Bullish Displacement: strong bullish candle with body significantly above local volatility
  int is_bullish_displacement;

  // Bearish Displacement: strong bearish candle with body significantly above local volatility
  int is_bearish_displacement;
 
  // ============================================================================
  // 8. FIBONACCI INPUT VARIABLES CALCULATION
  // ============================================================================
  
  // Support variables to search for swing high and swing low
  real swing_high_val;
  real swing_low_val;
  int lookback_window; 
  
  // Range of the main swing move (Swing Range)
  real swing_range;

  // Fibonacci Level Variables Calculation
  // For Uptrend (Retracement from Low to High):
  real fib_level_618;
  real fib_level_500;
  real fib_expansion_1618;
  
  // Price Action Support/Resistance Structure (structure_sr)
  // Defines the nearest structure as the broken high in an uptrend
  // or the broken low in a downtrend.
  real structure_sr;
  
  // Strategy 1: Confluence between Golden Ratio (61.8%) and S/R Structure
  // Tolerance proportional to local volatility (e.g., 15% of volatility)
  int is_fib_sr_confluence;

  // Strategy 2: Discount vs. Premium Zone Filter (50% Level)
  // In an uptrend (trend_dir == 1), looking to buy cheap (price below 50%)
  int is_discount_zone_bull;

  // In a downtrend (trend_dir == -1), looking to sell high (price above 50%)
  int is_premium_zone_bear;

  // Strategy 3: Target / Risk Projection
  // Identifies if current price is within reach of the 161.8% expansion target
  int is_fib_target_valid;
  
  // Evaluates Bearish Pivot (Trough)  
  int is_trough;
  
  int start_idx;
  
  vector[T] trend_dir;
  
  //  Conditions of the Moving Average and Price at time 't'
  int ma_bullish;
  int ma_bearish;

  //  Pivot Structure Conditions
  int pivots_bullish;
  int pivots_bearish;
  
  // ============================================================================
  // HANGING MAN PATTERN DETECTION
  // ============================================================================

  // Long lower wick (e.g., > 50% of the range)
  int has_long_lower_wick;
  
  // Small upper wick (e.g., ≤ 15% of the range)
  int has_small_upper_wick;
  
  // Small body (e.g., body less than or equal to 30% of the total range)
  int has_small_body;

  // Context Filter (Must be in Top / Premium zone)
  // Price closes in the upper half of the recent leg
  int is_in_premium_zone;

  // Logical Combination of the Hanging Man
  int is_hanging_man;
  
  // ===========================================
  // LIQUIDITY SWEEP / TURTLE SOUP DETECTION 
  // ===========================================

  // Pattern activation flag (0 = Not detected, 1 = Detected)
  int is_bearish_turtle_soup;
  int is_bullish_turtle_soup;
   
  // --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  RULES FOR DOUBLE TOP WITH FALSE BREAKOUT (BEARISH SWEEP)
  // --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  
  // a) The current high breaks the high of the previous pivot (High Liquidity Sweep)
  int high_swept_peak;
  
  // b) The close rejects the breakout and returns inside the previous range
  int close_below_peak;
  
  // c) Presence of a significant upper rejection wick (e.g., ≥ 35% of the candle)
  int has_rejection_upper_wick;

  // d) Context Filter: Occurs in the Premium Zone
  int is_in_premium;
  int is_bearish_sweep;

  // --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  RULES FOR DOUBLE BOTTOM WITH FALSE BREAKOUT (BULLISH SWEEP)
  // --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  
  // a) The current low breaks below the low of the previous pivot (Low Liquidity Sweep).
  int low_swept_trough;
  
  // b) The close rejects the low and closes above the previous low.
  int close_above_trough;
  
  // c) Presence of a significant lower rejection wick (e.g., ≥ 35% of the candle)
  int has_rejection_lower_wick;

  // d) Context Filter: Occurs in a Discount Zone
  int is_in_discount;
  int is_bullish_sweep;
  
  // ============================================================================  
  // 10. Indicadores de Dinâmica Microestrutural de Velas (ou Candle Anatomy Metrics)
  // ============================================================================  
  
  // PVI_T (Price Position Index): Indica onde o preço fechou em relação à amplitude total da barra [0 = mínima, 1 = máxima].
  // Serve para medir o controle relativo entre compradores e vendedores no encerramento do dia.
  real PVI_T;

  // Lower_Shadow_Ratio: Proporção da sombra inferior em relação à amplitude total da barra (0.0 a 1.0).
  // Quantifica o nível de rejeição de preços baixos e a força da absorção compradora na mínima.
  real Lower_Shadow_Ratio;

  // Upper_Shadow_Ratio: Proporção da sombra superior em relação à amplitude total da barra (0.0 a 1.0).
  // Quantifica o nível de rejeição de preços altos e a força da absorção vendedora na máxima.
  real Upper_Shadow_Ratio;
  
     // ============================================================================
   // STORMER SETUPS & REGIME SWITCH IDENTIFICATION
   // ============================================================================

   int<lower=0, upper=1> has_valid_candle_quality;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> has_strong_trend_regime;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> switch_active;

   // Setup 1: PFR
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_pfr_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_pfr_bearish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_pfr_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_pfr_bearish;

   // Setup 2: Dave Landry
   int<lower=0, upper=1> ema20_rising;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> ema20_falling;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_dave_landry_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_dave_landry_bearish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_dave_landry_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_dave_landry_bearish;

   // 123 Pattern
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_pattern_123_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> raw_pattern_123_bearish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_pattern_123_bullish;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_pattern_123_bearish;

   // Stormer Confluence Variables
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_stormer_confluence_bull;
   int<lower=0, upper=1> is_stormer_confluence_bear;

   // ===============================================================
   //       Mapping of Confluent Divergences (CVD & RSI)
   // ===============================================================
  
   // Isolating the reference window (N-11 to N-2)
   int window;
   real price_max_prev;
   real price_min_prev;  
   real rsi_max_prev;
   real rsi_min_prev;  
   real cvd_max_prev;
   real cvd_min_prev;
   
   // Bearish Divergence: Price hits higher high/equal high, but indicator stays lower
   int rsi_bear_recent;
   int cvd_bear_recent;
   // Bullish Divergence: Price hits lower low/equal low, but indicator stays higher
   int rsi_bull_recent;
   int cvd_bull_recent;
   
   // Confluence Flags
   int is_confluent_sell;
   int is_confluent_buy; 

   // ===============================================================
   //           NORMALIZED ELASTICITY (Z-Distance via ATR)
   // ===============================================================
  
   // Distance from the current price to the EMA200, measured in multiples of ATR.
   real ema_dist_atr;
   real abs_elasticity;

   // Elasticity Multiplier (Stretch Factor)
   // If the distance is > 2 ATRs, the price is stretched -> Expand the TP
   real elasticity_mult; 

   // ===================================
   //   Institutional S/R (Big Figures)
   // ===================================
   real big_figure_bull;
   real big_figure_bear; 
   real buffer_pips;
   int is_news_today; // (Tier 3 FOREX News for t=N)   


  // ============================================================================
  // 11. AGGREGATED SCORE SYSTEM (PA SCORE)
  // ============================================================================
  real pa_score_bull = 0.0;
  real pa_score_bear = 0.0;
     
  // ===========================================================
  // Directional Probabilities
  // ===========================================================
  /*
      Posterior probabilities of upward and downward price
      movement relative to the Tent market level.
  
         p_up   = P(Y_future > Y_Tent)
         p_down = P(Y_future < Y_Tent)
  */
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0> p_up;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0> p_down;
  real lambda_dofp_trend;
  real lambda_dofp_chop;
  real lambda_dofp;
  real logit_p_up_final;
  real pa_net_score;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0>  prob_pa_up;
  real weight_pa;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0>  p_up_pa;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0>  p_down_pa;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0>  p_up_final;
  real <lower=0.0, upper =1.0>  p_down_final;
  // ===========================================================
  // Risk-Reward and Bayesian Trading Edge Metrics
  // ===========================================================
  /*
      reward :
            Expected profit toward the target quantile.
  
      risk :
         Expected adverse excursion measured using
         Expected Shortfall.
  
      rr :
         Classical reward-to-risk ratio.
  
      ERR :
        Probability-weighted Expected Risk-Reward ratio.
  
        ERR > 1 implies that expected reward,
        adjusted by directional probability,
        exceeds expected risk.
  
      edge :
        Net directional advantage:
           edge = p_up_final - p_down_final
  
         Positive values favor long exposure.
        Negative values favor short exposure.
  */  
  real reward_long;
  real risk_long; 
  real rr_long;
  real reward_short;  
  real risk_short;
  real rr_short;
  real ERR_long;
  real ERR_short;
  real delta_ERR;
  real <lower=-1.0, upper = 1.0> edge;	
  /* 
      ==== Spike-and-slab mix indicator for future jump
           1 = slab (huge jump), 0 = spike (zero jump) ====  */
  int is_slab;
  /*
      Copy observed data to new vectors for prediction
  */ 
  h_gru_concatenate_new[1:T] = h_gru_concatenate;  
  R_new[1:T,1] = R[1:T,1];
  R_new[1:T,2] = R[1:T,2];
  sigma_observed_new[1:T,1] = sigma_observed[1:T,1];
  sigma_observed_new[1:T,2] = sigma_observed[1:T,2];
  total_uncertainty_prior_new[1:T,1] = total_uncertainty_prior[1:T,1];
  total_uncertainty_posterior_new[1:T,1] = total_uncertainty_posterior[1:T,2];
  //
  for (t in 1:T) { 
    // Regime probabilities (filtered-posterior) 
    regime_probs[t] = softmax(log_alpha[t])'; 
    /* 
	     ==== Compute mu/sigma per regime ====    */		 
    for (k in 1:2) {
      real mu_k = x_p[t, k][1];
      real sigma_k = sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_prior[t, k]));
      // Prior regime probability
      if (t == 1) {
        log_prior[k] = log(0.5);  
      } else {
        vector[2] tmp;
        for (j in 1:2) {
          tmp[j] = log_alpha[t-1, j] + log(transition_matrix[j, k]);
        }
        log_prior[k] = log_sum_exp(tmp);
      }
    //  Conditional log-likelihood under regime k
    log_mix_density[k] = log_prior[k] +
                  student_t_lpdf(y[t] | nu, mu_k, sigma_k);
    }
    // Marginal predictive log-likelihood (mixture over regimes)
    log_lik[t] = log_sum_exp(log_mix_density);
    // =================================
    //    Posterior Predictive Check 
    // =================================
	prob_next[t,1] = regime_probs[t, 1] * transition_matrix[1, 1] + regime_probs[t, 2] * transition_matrix[2, 1];
    prob_next[t,2] = regime_probs[t, 1] * transition_matrix[1, 2] + regime_probs[t, 2] * transition_matrix[2, 2];
    
    regime_in_sample = categorical_rng(softmax(log_alpha[t]));
    if (regime_in_sample == 1) {
       mu[t] = x_est[t, 1][1];
       sigma[t] = sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_posterior[t,1]));
    } else {
       mu[t] = x_est[t, 2][1];
       sigma[t] = sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_posterior[t,2]));
    } 
	
	// ===========================
    //  Posterior predictive draw
	// ===========================
    y_sim[t] = student_t_rng(nu, mu[t], sigma[t]);
  }
  //
  prob_next[T+1,1] = regime_probs[T, 1] * transition_matrix[1, 1] + regime_probs[T, 2] * transition_matrix[2, 1];
  prob_next[T+1,2] = regime_probs[T, 1] * transition_matrix[1, 2] + regime_probs[T, 2] * transition_matrix[2, 2];
  //
  p_chop_last = softmax(log_alpha[T])[2];
  {
    //    Recalculate consolidation gating for the Tent state (smooth threshold)
    real ou_gate_N = fmin(fmax((p_chop_last - 0.6) / 0.4, 0.0), 1.0);
    
    //    Effective speed of the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) mean-reversion process
    //    Indexing phi[2] solves the 'right-hand side type = vector' mismatch
    theta_eff = ou_gate_N * phi[2];

    //    Half-life calculation in D1 bar/day units
    if (theta_eff > 1e-6) {
      half_life = log(2.0) / theta_eff;
    } else {
    /*
               ===== SAFETY GUARD VALUE (STRONG TREND REGIME): ===== 
     
       When theta_eff approaches zero (ou_gate -> 0), the market is in a strong, 
       directional trend. In this regime, there is no active mean-reversion 
       force pulling the price back toward the EMAs. 
        
       Setting half_life to 999.0 acts as a numerical fallback representing an 
       infinitely slow decay (no expectation of reverting to the fast/slow EMAs)
       while preventing division-by-zero errors in Stan.
       
	*/
    half_life = 999.0;
    }
  }
  /*			
	              ==== Conditional mean iterations for forecasting at t= T+1 ====
	                                  Simulating new jumps 
  */
  is_slab = bernoulli_rng(mean(pi_z));
  if (is_slab == 1) {
      jumps_new = normal_rng(0, sigma_slab);
   } else {
      jumps_new = normal_rng(0, sigma_spike);
  }
  //
  /*   
	                      ===== GRU momentum update for t= T+1 ===== 
  */
  v_g_f_gru_forward_new = (momentum * v_g_f_gru_forward[T] + epsilon*thnw_f_gru_scalar_forward*h_gru_forward[T]);
  g_f_gru_forward_new =   inv_logit(v_g_f_gru_forward_new + thnv_f_gru_scalar_forward*y[T] + thnb_f_gru_scalar_forward);
  v_h_gru_forward_new =   (momentum *v_h_gru_forward[T] + epsilon*thnw_h_gru_scalar_forward*(g_f_gru_forward_new + h_gru_forward[T]));
  h_gru_forward_new =     tanh(v_h_gru_forward_new + theta_h_gru_scalar_forward*y[T] + thnb_h_gru_scalar_forward);
  h_gru_forward_new_updated = (1- g_f_gru_forward_new)*h_gru_forward[T] +  g_f_gru_forward_new*h_gru_forward_new; 
  /*  
	                       For the backward GRU in forecasting:
		
     Freezing the last backward hidden state.This is the most common approach for practical forecasting with B-RNNs.
     Using the last computed backward state from obsezed data
		 
  */
  h_gru_concatenate_new[T+1] = (h_gru_backward_updated[T] + h_gru_forward_new_updated)/2;
  //
  // Base volatility + cumulative impact of scheduled news
  
  R_new[T+1,1] = intercept_r[1] +  beta_dailly[1]*rv[T] + phix_residuals[1]*var_residuals[T,1] + beta_weekly[1]*rv[T-4] + beta_monthly[1] * rv[T-21]
                + beta_usa[1]*news_usa_tomorrow + beta_eur[1]*news_eur_tomorrow + gamma_geo[1] * news_geo_tomorrow; 				

  R_new[T+1,2] = intercept_r[2] + beta_dailly[2]*rv[T]  + phix_residuals[2]*var_residuals[T,2] + beta_weekly[2]*rv[T-4] + beta_monthly[2] * rv[T-21]
                + beta_usa[2]*news_usa_tomorrow + beta_eur[2]*news_eur_tomorrow + gamma_geo[2] * news_geo_tomorrow; 	 
  
  sigma_observed_new[T+1,1] = ReLU(R_new[T+1,1] * (1 + kappa[1] * square(jumps_new)));  
  sigma_observed_new[T+1,2] = ReLU(R_new[T+1,2] * (1 + kappa[2] * square(jumps_new)));  
  
  // 

  x_next_regime1 = predict_ukf(x_est[T, 1], P_est[T, 1],  phi_gru[1], h_gru_concatenate_new[T+1],theta_ma[1],
                   phi[1] , mu_long[T,1], ou_shape[1], 1.0-p_chop_last , beta_vol[1], innovation_prev[T,1],liquidity_grad_t[T],beta_liq[1],beta_jump[1],D_breakout[T],scalp_index_t[T],
				   ema_signal_t[T], ema_compression_t[T] ,sigma_observed[T,1]); 
  
  x_next_regime2 = predict_ukf(x_est[T, 2], P_est[T, 2], phi_gru[2], h_gru_concatenate_new[T+1],theta_ma[2], phi[2] , 
		           mu_long[T,2], ou_shape[2], p_chop_last, beta_vol[2], innovation_prev[T,2],liquidity_grad_t[T],beta_liq[2],beta_jump[2],D_breakout[T],scalp_index_t[T],
				   ema_signal_t[T], ema_compression_t[T] ,sigma_observed[T,2]);
				   
  // Trending regime 
  A_new_regime1[1,1] = 1.0; 
  A_new_regime1[1,2] = 1.2; 
  
  A_new_regime1[2,1] = 0.0; 
  A_new_regime1[2,2] = 0.95; 
  
  // Chop (mean-reverting regime)
  A_new_regime2[1,1] = 0.8; 
  A_new_regime2[1,2] = 0.3; 
  
  A_new_regime2[2,1] = 0.0; 
  A_new_regime2[2,2] = 0.25; 
  
  // Covariance propagation 
  P_p_new_regime1 = A_new_regime1 * P_est[T,1] * A_new_regime1' + Q; 
  P_p_new_regime2 = A_new_regime2 * P_est[T,2] * A_new_regime2' + Q;
  
  total_uncertainty_prior_new[T+1,1] = P_p_new_regime1[1,1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,1];
  total_uncertainty_prior_new[T+1,2] = P_p_new_regime2[1,1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,2];
  
  S_new_regime1 = P_p_new_regime1[1, 1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,1]; 
  S_new_regime2 = P_p_new_regime2[1, 1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,2]; 

  // Kalman Gain  
  K_regime1[1] = P_p_new_regime1[1,1] / S_new_regime1;
  K_regime1[2] = P_p_new_regime1[2,1] / S_new_regime1;

  K_regime2[1] = P_p_new_regime2[1,1] / S_new_regime2;
  K_regime2[2] = P_p_new_regime2[2,1] / S_new_regime2;
  
  // Covariance Update 
  
  P_post_new_regime1 = (I - K_regime1 * H') * P_p_new_regime1 * (I - K_regime1 * H')'
                        + K_regime1 * sigma_observed_new[T+1,1] * K_regime1';

  P_post_new_regime2 = (I - K_regime2 * H') * P_p_new_regime2 * (I - K_regime2 * H')'
                        + K_regime2 * sigma_observed_new[T+1,2] * K_regime2';
						
  total_uncertainty_posterior_new[T+1,1] = P_post_new_regime1[1,1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,1];
  total_uncertainty_posterior_new[T+1,2] = P_post_new_regime2[1,1] + sigma_observed_new[T+1,2];
  
  // Simulating new residuals  
  innovation_regime1 = student_t_rng(nu, 0.0, sqrt(sigma_observed_new[T+1,1]));
  innovation_regime2 = student_t_rng(nu, 0.0, sqrt(sigma_observed_new[T+1,2])); 

  // Update/Simulation
  x_est_final_new[1] = x_next_regime1 + K_regime1 * innovation_regime1;
  x_est_final_new[2] = x_next_regime2 + K_regime2 * innovation_regime2;  

  if (regime_in_sample == 1) {
       mu[T+1] = x_est_final_new[1][1];
       sigma[T+1] = sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_posterior_new[T+1,1]));
    } else {
       mu[T+1] = x_est_final_new[2][1];
       sigma[T+1] = sqrt(ReLU(total_uncertainty_posterior_new[T+1,2]));
  }  		

  prob_trend = prob_next[T+1,1];
  prob_scalp = prob_next[T+1,2];

  // =====================
  //   TP/SL multipliers
  // =====================
    
  k_tp = prob_trend * k_tp_trend + prob_scalp * k_tp_scalp;
  k_sl = prob_trend * k_sl_trend + prob_scalp * k_sl_scalp;
  
  // =============================
  // Quantiles for Stop / Target
  // =============================

  q_std_low  = student_t_quantile(0.05, nu);
  q_std_high = student_t_quantile(0.95, nu);

  // Left tail (risk side)
  q_low  = mu[T+1] + sigma[T+1] * q_std_low;  // 5% VaR: lower tail quantile (lowest downside risk threshold)  
  
  // Right tail (upside potential)
  q_high = mu[T+1] + sigma[T+1] * q_std_high;  // 95% quantile: upper tail (extreme upside move)

  ES_up = expected_shortfall_t(mu[T+1], sigma[T+1], nu, 0.95);   // ES 95% superior
  ES_down = expected_shortfall_t(mu[T+1], sigma[T+1], nu, 0.05); // ES 5% inferior 

  // ============================================================================
  // 1. BASE DATA AND DIMENTIONS FOR THE TENT CANDLE (N) AND PREVIOUS ONES
  // ============================================================================

  candle_range_N = high[T] - low[T] + 1e-8;
  body_N         = fabs(y[T] - open[T]);
  body_N1        = fabs(y[T-1] - open[T-1]);
  body_N2        = fabs(y[T-2] - open[T-2]);

  range_N1       = high[T-1] - low[T-1] + 1e-8;
  upper_wick_N   = high[T] - fmax(y[T], open[T]);
  lower_wick_N   = fmin(y[T], open[T]) - low[T];

  // Local Reference Volatility (Standard Deviation)
  local_vol = sigma[T] + 1e-8;  // To avoid look ahead bias by using sigma at t=T

  // ============================================================================
  // 2. WICK METRICS AND NORMALIZED MOMENTUM
  // ============================================================================
  // Note: Ensure you pass High and Low in the Stan 'data' block

  lower_wick_ratio = lower_wick_N / candle_range_N;
  upper_wick_ratio = upper_wick_N / candle_range_N;

  candle_body_norm      = (y[T] - open[T]) / local_vol;
  prev_candle_body_norm = (y[T-1] - open[T-1]) / local_vol;
  pa_reversal_momentum  = candle_body_norm - prev_candle_body_norm;

  // ============================================================================
  // 3. PRICE ACTION STRUCTURES - BULLISH
  // ============================================================================

  // A. Morning Star Pattern
  is_morning_star = (
       (y[T-2] < open[T-2]) &&                           // N-2 is bearish
       (body_N1 / range_N1 < 0.30) &&                    // N-1 is small body/Doji
       (y[T] > open[T]) &&                               // N is bullish
       (y[T] > (open[T-2] + y[T-2]) / 2.0)               // N closes above Nhe midpoint of N-2
  ) ? 1 : 0;

  // B. Bullish Engulfing
  is_bullish_engulfing = (
       (y[T-1] < open[T-1]) &&                           // N-1 was bearish
       (y[T] > open[T]) &&                               // N is bullish
       (y[T] >= open[T-1]) &&                            // N close exceeds N-1 open
       (open[T] <= y[T-1])                               // N open is at or below N-1 close
  ) ? 1 : 0;

  // C. Hammer (Pinbar Bottom)
  is_hammer = (
       (lower_wick_ratio >= 0.60) &&                     // Lower wick >= 60% of total range
       (upper_wick_ratio <= 0.10) &&                     // Upper wick <= 10%
       (body_N / candle_range_N <= 0.30)                 // Small body <= 30%
  ) ? 1 : 0;

  // D. Bullish Break of Structure (BOS)
  max_high_5 = max(high[T-5:T-1]);
  is_bos_bullish = (
       (y[T] > max_high_5) && 
       (y[T-1] <= max_high_5)
  ) ? 1 : 0;

  // E. Bullish Liquidity Sweep (Low Rejection)
  min_low_5 = min(low[T-5:T-1]);
  is_liquidity_sweep_bullish = (
       (low[T] < min_low_5) &&                           // Pierced recent swing low
       (y[T] > min_low_5) &&                             // Closed back above support
       (lower_wick_ratio > 0.40)                         // Significant lower wick rejection
  ) ? 1 : 0;

  // F. Bullish Order Block
  body_N1_norm = body_N1 / local_vol;
  is_bullish_order_block = (
       (y[T-2] < open[T-2]) &&                           // N-2 was bearish
       (y[T-1] > open[T-1]) &&                           // N-1 bullish displacement
       (body_N1_norm > 1.5) &&                           // Volatility expansion above average
       (low[T] <= high[T-2]) &&                          // Retests top of the Order Block
       (y[T] > low[T-2])                                 // Respects the bottom of the block
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // G. Bullish Fair Value Gap (FVG)
   fvg_bullish_size = fmax(0.0, low[T] - high[T-2]);
   fvg_bullish_norm = fvg_bullish_size / local_vol;

   // H. Piercing Line
   is_piercing_line = (
       (y[T-1] < open[T-1]) &&                           // N-1 is bearish
       (y[T] > open[T]) &&                               // N is bullish
       (open[T] <= low[T-1]) &&                          // Open at or below N-1 low
       (y[T] >= open[T-1] - (body_N1 * 0.50)) &&         // Penetrates > 50% into N-1 body
       (y[T] < open[T-1])                                // Does noN fully engulf
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // I. Selling Climax (Exhaustion)
   body_N_norm = body_N / local_vol;
   is_selling_climax = (
       (y[T] < open[T]) && 
       (body_N_norm > 2.5) && 
       (RSI[T] < 30)
   ) ? 1 : 0;
   
   last_swing_high =  max(high[T-10:T-2]);
   
   is_choch_bullish = (
    y[T] > last_swing_high &&
    y[T-1] <= last_swing_high
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // ============================================================================
   // 4. PRICE ACTION STRUCTURES - BEARISH
   // ============================================================================

   // A. Evening Star Pattern
   is_evening_star = (
     (y[T-2] > open[T-2]) &&                           // N-2 is bullish
     (body_N1 / range_N1 < 0.30) &&                    // N-1 is small body/Doji
     (y[T] < open[T]) &&                               // N is bearish
     (y[T] < (open[T-2] + y[T-2]) / 2.0)               // N closes below Nhe midpoint of N-2
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // B. Shooting Star (Upper Wick RejecNion)
   is_shooting_star = (
     (upper_wick_ratio >= 0.60) &&                     // Upper wick >= 60% of total range
     (lower_wick_ratio <= 0.10) &&                     // Lower wick <= 10%
     (body_N / candle_range_N <= 0.30)                 // Small body <= 30%
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // C. Bearish Change of Character (CHOCH)
   last_swing_low = min(low[T-10:T-2]);
   is_choch_bearish = (
     (y[T] < last_swing_low) && 
     (y[T-1] >= last_swing_low)
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // D. Bearish Breaker Block
   is_bearish_breaker = (
     (y[T-3] > open[T-3]) &&                           // N-3 was bullish (former supporN)
     (y[T-1] < low[T-3])  &&                           // N-1 strongly violated N-3 low
     (high[T] >= low[T-3]) &&                          // N retests violated support
     (y[T] <= high[T-3])                               // Respects top of the block
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // E. Bearish Fair Value Gap (FVG)
   fvg_bearish_size = fmax(0.0, low[T-2] - high[T]);
   fvg_bearish_norm = fvg_bearish_size / local_vol;

   // F. Buying Climax (ExhausNion)
   is_buying_climax = (
       (y[T] > open[T]) && 
       (body_N_norm > 2.5) && 
       (RSI[T] > 70)
   ) ? 1 : 0;

   // ============================================================================
   // 5. NEUNRAL STRUCTURES / LIQUIDITY CONTEXT
   // ============================================================================

   is_inside_bar = (high[T] <= high[T-1] && low[T] >= low[T-1]) ? 1 : 0;
   is_nr4        = (candle_range_N < min(high[T-3:T-1] - low[T-3:T-1])) ? 1 : 0;

   eq_threshold  = 0.10 * local_vol;
   is_equal_highs = (fabs(high[T-1] - high[T-2]) < eq_threshold) ? 1 : 0;
   is_equal_lows  = (fabs(low[T-1] - low[T-2]) < eq_threshold) ? 1 : 0;
